A Short Profile and Philosophy of Shri SBPBK Satyanarayana Rao

-- Justice P. Kodanda Ramayya

Sri SBPBK was born in a Zamindar family of Kapileswarapuram in East Godavari District on 23.09.1921. His full name is Sreebalusu Prabhakara Buchi Krishna Satyanarayana Rao. He was graduated from the Presidency College, Madras in 1944 and has a record of 60 years of public life covering all fields of social and cultural activities and occupying higher posts. He was the chairman of Zilla Parishad for 12 years and Member of A.P. Legislative Council representing North Circar Districts Graduates Constituency for two terms in 1958 and 1980. He was Member of Andhra University Syndicate from 1981 to 1984. He was a Member of A.P. Sangeetha Nataka Academy from 1981 to 84. He was Chairman of Srisailam Devasthanam Trust Board from 1977 to 1980. A pioneer of industrial development in sugar manufacture and textiles along with soft drinks and IMFL since 1955 upto date. Founder President of S.R.V.B.S.J.B. Maharani College, Peddapuram since 1967. He established Veda Pathasala and Hari Katha Pathasala at Kapileswarapuram since 1973 upto date. He got Vijaya Ratna by the International Friendship Society of India, New Delhi. He was Elected Member of Lok Sabha from Rajahmundry constituency. Served as Minister for State for Agriculture for one year and there after continued as Member of Parliament for the rest of the term i.e., 13.10.1999 to 7.02.2004.

During the term he was the Chairman of Zilla Parishad, he made several developments in the education of girls and tribal and backward areas and extra curricular activities like songs and dramas. It was considered to be a golden era. He took initiative for starting Kritiventi Perraju Panthulu Junior Collge at Ramachandrapuram and also Agricultural Polytechnic at Ramachandrapuram in his name. He got several moral instruction and also teaching of spiritual lessons printed and supplied to all secondary schools in the district.

During Gandhi Centenary Celebrations, he organized Essay Writing Competition for the students of High Schools in the District and for those students who have come out successful in the competitions, he also organized visit of all important places like Sabarmathi Ashram, Wardha, Birla Mandir, etc. places connected with Gandhiji’s life and also made available the meeting of Smt. Indira Gandhi who had appreciated their visit and blessed them.

Tracing the beginning of irrigation systems in Andhra Pradesh, he got details of the life of Sri ARTHUR COTTON who built the Godavari and Krishna Ayacuts nearly 130 years ago fully investigated and got his biography published to commemorate his memory. This book is considered to be a standard book of its kind by the Irrigation Department. Sri Satyanarayana Rao was affectionately called as “UPADHYAYA BANDHU”. Another activity of irrigation is when there is a breach to the Godavari Bund in the year 1953 and 1956 at Kapileswarapuram and Kulla, the services rendered to flood victims by Sri Rao were appreciated by Sri Chandulal Trivedi, the then Governor of Andhra Pradesh.

During the Pakistan war, East Godavari District secured No.1 position in collecting donations to war fund and gold under his able leadership and the then Prime Minister, Lal Bahadur Sastry made a special visit to the District and personally received the gold collected from Sri Satyanarayana Rao.

During his capacity as M.P., he utilized Constituency Development Fund and other Development Funds to a tune of 12.17 crores for Rural Development taking about 5 to 600 works towards creating infrastructure like roads and bridges and helping the poor SCs, STs and BCs and other Minorities.

‘‘In Industries, he got employed nearly 15000 people directly or indirectly. For such innovative work, he was honoured with the title of ‘VIJAYA RATNA’.’’

On the cultural activities, besides starting Harikatha Patasala and Veda Patasala at Kapileswarapuram, he distributed 18 Lalita Kala Puraskarams and 18 Sahitya Puraskarams giving outstanding persons in those fields respectively. He loved all those persons as members of the family and they in their turn considered that they are the members of his family. That is how he loved people and people loved him.

He was born in a family of Zamindars, a noble family and his actions and utterances are nobler and ideal for other contemporaries to follow. We can call him ‘Raja Satyanarayana’.

He has got a great regard for his father Sri B.C. Sarvaraya, a majestic personality with his turban and hence, he established a number of institutions in his name, both industrial, financial and educational.

His brother Sri B.P. Pattabhirama Rao occupied the post of Minister of State for Industries for some time and also for Finance for some time during the period when Smt. Indira Gandhi was the Prime Minister.

Subhashitha Ratna Mala

Sri S.B.P.B.K. published Subhashitha Ratna Mala in nine volumes published by Sarvaraya Sugars covering different religions

  • Bhagavat Gita
  • Utterances of Sankara Bhagavat Pada
  • Utterances of Goutama Budha
  • Valmiki
  • Christ
  • Maha Bharata by Veda Vyasa
  • Prophet Mohammed
  • Sri Ramanuja
  • Sri Nanak

Thus, he has established the idea of integration of all religions together, in this multi-religious country of ours. In his units of industry and even in cultural units, he employed all people of Christians, Muslims, Dalits and Non-Dalits as he has imbibed the norm of this Vedic religion, which is not a religion but a way of life. Thus, he helped for the integration of the nation very much in his activity and also in his utterances

Sri S.B.P.B.K. has a deep knowledge of Sruti, Smruthi, Purana and Itihasas. He published 20 to 25 books of authors of great erudite scholarship of ancient times and himself read them intensively and understood the philosophy behind those utterances. Accordingly, he got abiding faith in the utterance of Sage Veda Vyasa, the author of Maha Bharata, who said, “I will disclose a great secret – there is no position superior to that of humanity”, and hence, every intelligent man should try to acquire liberation of the soul, which is known as Atma Siddhi by Veda, as man alone is endowed with the faculty of acquiring liberation of soul. For that, one should follow the concept of Dharma, a duty oriented, which involves sacrifice and said that man should follow five concepts of ‘Dharmam, Srutam, Tapas, Diksha, Vyavasayam’ i.e., earn money on proper way, study subject, concentrate on work, take a owe having an object of establishing institutions of permanent value and for that purpose constantly make effort which is last thing called as Vyavasaya. He did all these things with such a dedicated mind that he created a source for Dharmic work.

Unlike Pandit Madan Mohan Malaviya who declared that ‘I am the greatest beggar’, and secured donations from several persons, Sri S.B.P.B.K. secured a source for Dharmik work himself, and used the same for Dharmik work. For this work of Pravruthi Marga, he established number of units -industrial and financial and the social and cultural work established by him constantly grew from strength to strength in view of the permanent source of wealth from those units. Hence he believes strongly that surplus money is not his and it is intended for distribution and he did accordingly.

Finally, we can sum up his philosophy in life. One conspicuous feature of his life is, he has no children. Himself or his Dharmapatni Smt. Rajarajeswaramma do not have any such feeling of the absence of progeny at any time. They both accept with full faith the utterance of the Sruti that every human being should attain immortality by practicing Pravruthi Dharma initially, by working vigorously to secure wealth and in the end of life he should attain immortality by sacrificing/discarding all activities, progeny and wealth. Not by work, not by progeny, not by wealth, by renunciation alone one attains immortality – tayagenaikenamrtatva manasuh. Sri S.B.P.B.K. followed these concepts and ended his life with total sacrifice.

He lived 90 years and his 60 years career clearly warrants a posthumous national award to be given to his personality by the Union of India.

Srimad Ramayana & Mahabharatam,
Our National Epics Promote Integration Of Our Nation

" Narayanam Namaskrutya Naramchaiva Narothamam
Devim Saraswatheem Vyasam Tato Jaya Mudeerayeth

Veda Vyasa Gunavasa Vidyadisa Satamvasa
Mam Nirasam Gataklesam Kurvanasam Harenisam

Chandrasekharendra Saraswathi Guru Devo Nama: "

We have two Itihasas – One Srimad Ramayana and another Sri Mahabharata.

These two are heroic histories of our country. But they have definite message for the humanity. Ramayana is verily the Veda transmuted into Kavya by Sage Valmiki. Sri Mahabharata is known as 5th Veda containing the essence of Vedas and experience of sages who practiced those concepts. Hence both of them embody the concepts mentioned in Veda. Hence, we have to correlate the vedic hymns with the concepts given in these two epics by sages. The message of both these epics is the same, namely, man’s duty to perform Dharma in life. Ramayana says, ‘Dharmasaram Idam Jagat’ from Dharma follows wealth, form Dharma comes happiness. By recourse to Dharma, one gets everything. Na sukhath Labyate sukham. Dharma does not follow from merriment.

The epic Maha Bharata is the biggest in its composition, seven times as great as the Iliad and Odyssey combined, animating the hearts of Indians over thousands of years, which will continue to do so in future.

Sage Veda Vyasa, herein after called, Maharsi, himself given the message of this epic at the end of this magnificent work. It is named as Bharata Savitri.

He Said,
  • Thousands of mothers, and fathers, and hundreds of sons and wives arise in the world and depart from it. Others will arise and similarly go away. There are thousands of occasions for joy and hundreds of occasions for fears. These affect only him who is ignorant but never him that is wise”
  • With uplifted arms I am crying aloud, but nobody hears me. From Dharma originate profit and pleasure. Why should not Dharma, therefore, be sought? For the sake of pleasure or fear, or cupidity, or even for life one should not forsake Dharma
  • Dharma is eternal. Pleasure and pain are not eternal. Jiva is eternal. The cause, however, of Jivas being invested with a body is not eternal.”

If we know the explicit and implicit meaning of this message, we will be knowing fully the scheme of his work.

Maharsi Veda Vyasa said, “I will disclose a great secret – there is no position superior to that of humanity”. Hence, he said, every intelligent man should try to acquire liberation of the soul, which is known as Atma Siddhi by Veda, as man alone is endowed with the faculty of acquiring liberation of soul. Veda is the source of all Dharma. It prescribes the means for Atma Siddhi in two ways known as Pravrtti and Nivrtti Dharma and prescribes the mode of practicing the same.

The concept of Dharma is a duty oriented. It involves sacrifice. The summery thereof:-
  • Pravrthi Dhrama involves sacrificing in some measure’ Nivrthi Dharma total sacrifice’. One in a million alone can follow it. Pravrthi Dharma must be followed by all.
  • Maharshi examined all Dhramas mentioned in Sruti, Smrti, Puranas and also Acharas (traditional concepts of Dharma).
  • Maharshi said ‘one should practice 3 essential dharmas- Dama, Daya, Dana. Keeping oneself within the bounds is Dama. Self control is its essence. Daya is compassion. Dana is charity.
  • If you fail to practice these three, you will meet with opposites – kama, krodha, lobha.- they are door ways of hell.
  • How to practice these dharmas?;- Yagna, Dana, Tapas.
    • 1. Yagna means total sacrifice,
    • 2. Dana is charities lesser degree,
    • 3. Tapas is
      • Meditation,
      • Concentration of work,
      • Doing one’s own duty

      • The first two for the society and the 3rd is for the individual.

  • Maharshi said the practice of dharma must be intensive, and hence one should without transgressing Dharma acquire srutham–science; tapas – concentration; diksa – vow; and vyavasayam - effort. One should become a first class citizen in any field he works. Maharsi said, affluence, prosperity, modesty, contentment and fame reside in skill and labour, not in idleness (MB-12-27-32).
  • You must acquire knowledge through physical science, moral science and spiritual science. Through knowledge of physical science – a study of external world, you will get money and gratify your senses and you also get awareness of several things in the world. Through moral science – a study of internal world, it gives virtue like dama, which is the basic concept for all Dharmas. Through spiritual science man gets integrated and knows how to lead an ideal manhood.
  • Nasti Siddhih Akarmanah – no one gets success unless works intensively.
  • A very significant lesson we must know in this context. That is Virat created dharma after creating beings for their well being. A direct text from Brhadranyaka Upanisad is as, follows (I, iv. 14) Yet he did not flourish. He specially projected that excellent form, dharma (righteousness). This dharma is the controller of the Ksatriaya. Therefore there is nothing higher than that. So even a weak man hopes to defeat a stronger man through dharma as one contending with the king. That dharma is verily truth, Therefore they say about a person speaking truth, ‘He speaks of dharma’ or about a person speaking of dharma, ‘He speaks of truth,’ for both these are but dharma.

      We note here two things:

    • Dharma is akin to rule of law which binds all persons including the ruler.
    • Dharma and truth are the same, so they are inter changeable words. Hence Yatho darmah thatho jayah- wherever there is darma, there lies success. Similarly Satyameva jayathe- truth also triumphs but not falsehood.
  • Dharmo Viswasya Jagatahprathista - dharma supports the universe. It is supreme means of the liberation. A significant thing is all beings protect their children. That is how the activity in the world is carried on. See the hen, a pigeon, a cow – it licks and licks the calf and make her clean. There is no reciprocity on the part of their offsprings towards their parents. A stud bull cannot recognize their mother cow. In villages they prevent it. But, there is no prohibition for it. The obligation of reciprocity is enjoined only on human beings. Dharma, the righteousness is the origin of mankind. Dharma is the nector of the gods. After leaving this body, man enjoys, through Dharma, the eternal felicity (MB-12-193-33). The gods without any exception have to take birth as mortals on earth if they violate dharma. Likewise, all mortals succeed in acquiring the dignity of gods by practicing dharma (MB-12-258-41).
  • We got political freedom and with that, we also should get social responsibility. In democracy, a citizen passes the law as a sovereign, as a subject he should obey it. On the other hand, after independence, we see increase of indiscipline, law breaking, crime, sex and drug, explosions are increasing. Hence, Swami Ranganathananda emphasized that there should be spiritual orientation of our democracy. Freedom, without citizenship qualities, is dangerous and harmful to the humanity and to the globe as a whole.

Infirmities of democracy:

  • Plato in his work ‘Republic’ described four types of unjust societies: timocracy, oligarchy or plutocracy, democracy and tyranny or despotism, in ascending order from least unjust to the most unjust. With reference to democracy, he described democracy as the third worst state. He said: It will result into a debtor nation, people borrow at high interest from the oligarchy, they will suffer bankruptcy. All people receive equal rights. They lack discipline. Politicians with no training will lead the public. They go on catering to the whims of the people. Like oligarchy, it lacks unity because people work for selfish motives instead of for the good of the state. However, we see, in the absence of other alternative, we have to resort to democracy. However, if the cardinal principles of virtue mentioned by Socrates are imbibed, democracy will be the happiest reign.

Ills we have experienced in this Age:

  • It is in democracy, Socrates, the founder of democracy, was executed. It is in democracy, Abraham Lincoln whose definition of democracy is ever lasting was killed. It is in democracy we killed Father of our Nation, Indira Gandhi, a dynamic personality, Rajiv Gandhi, a Gem, known as Mr. Clean.
  • We have embodied in our Constitution, equality, liberty and fraternity. They are aimed at protecting the citizen against any State action. People mistake them as political doctrines. On the other hand, Sri Aurobindo said, they are the eternal attributes of spirit and the practical recognition of the truth. How it is, I shall show when I come to definition of truth.
  • Equality is a mandate against the State, but not against co-citizen. The liberty we enjoy is really empty and unreal. Even married couple cannot go freely during night, let alone the unmarried girls. Fraternity is not even claimed to be enforceable principle. A society that aims at equality must sacrifice liberty. Similarly, a society which aims at liberty, must sacrifice equality. A man or society, so long they live in individual or communal ego, strife comes. The only remedy is the fraternity, which cannot be enforced in a court of law. However, our constitution was amended enumerating duties also indicating that the court can interpret rights in the light of those duties. Hence, we have to resort to the concept of Dharma, a practice of which by all citizens, promote cordiality among themselves.
  • Let us see what Socrates, the founder of democracy, said about the basic principles of democracy and how they should be practiced. He enunciated three basic principles for establishing an ideal state.
    • The first is dividing the society into three classes of citizens, rulers, auxiliaries, and craftsmen; making them to believe in a grand and noble life of myth of metals – gold, silver & iron.
    • The second principle is citizens should practice the four cardinal principles of virtue – wisdom, courage, discipline and justice.
    • The third principle is the ruler should be a philosopher.
  • Let us see how these two Epics embody these principles. So far the division of society is concerned, in none of the Greek cities the division is not accepted. Socrates discussed these principles with his colleagues. They objected it saying it is a most impracticable thing.
  • So far in our country, sages envisaged that the activity of the man should promote welfare of the community and the conceived the Varna and Asrama Dharmas. All varnas must sacrifice. The work of each varna is considered to be yajna.
    • Japa Yajna for the Brahmin. His strength lies in securing spiritual bliss.
    • Alamba Yajna for the ksatriya. His strength lies in protecting the people.
    • Havir Yajna for the Vaisya. His strength lies in securing richness to the country.
    • Paricharya Yajna for the last order, and he is known as karma vajrah, and his strength lies in executing several projects, including all yajnas in the country. There are varnas in Devas also. Angirasas are Brahmins. Adityas are Kshatriyas. Marutas are vysyas. Aswinis are Sudras. Those are all offices. There are no Asramas for devas.
  • The four asramas are intended for individual benefit.
    • Bhrahmacarya is for study, service, chastity, obedience and austerity.
    • Household Asrama to serve not only the humanity but also all created beings.
    • The third asrama aims that one should recede slowly from active life and make self enquiry - vana prastha – dweller of forest.
    • The fourth asrama involves total renunciation, which is optional.
  • Maharshi said this varna and asrama systems will be disregarded in subsequent ages due to jealousy as contemplated by the colleagues of Socrates. However, Sage said, let the last order rule the country, but all varnas should observe 9 minimum dharmas. They are,
    • control of anger,
    • truthfulness of speech,
    • wealth sharing,
    • forbearance,
    • begetting children upon one’s own married wife,
    • purity of conduct,
    • avoidance of quarrels,
    • simplicity,
    • maintenance of dependents,
    The ruler must follow the special dharma known as raja neeti.
  • The four Dharmas mentioned by Socrates are highlighted by sages in both these Epics.
    • Wisdom – So far as Socrates is concerned, he said, this virtue must be practiced by the ruling class. But, Maharshi said, it is a requisite for humanity - for success in this life as well as thereafter.
    • Courage – So far as this concept is concerned, Socrates said, it should be practiced by soldiers. Maharshi said, it is a requisite for all human beings, both in Pravruthi & Nivruthi Dharmas.
    • Self control – so far this third requirement is concerned, the translators of Plato’s ‘Republic’ gave different names. Some say, it is a discipline. Some say, tolerance. Cliffs Notes on Greek Classics mentioned it, as virtually self-control. Socrates said, it is a discipline in the State as a sort of ‘natural harmony’ or ‘agreement’ between the different sections of the community, where the wiser section rules, and the other sections agree to being ruled. So, to avoiding strife, he says, the virtue operates as a harmony among the community. But, if you classify it as a self-control compendiously, let us see what Maharshi said, which we call it Dama
    • Justice - So far as the fourth virtue, justice, is concerned, Socrates said, it is an important virtue and it lies at the root of all other virtues stating that nobody can be wise, courageous or disciplined unless he is also just. He summed it up as a virtue, which enables him to practice other virtues and also described it as goodness. He says that Justice is the quality that makes the existence of other virtues possible. From this, Socrates derives the principle that every man should have one job and one alone so that there may not be any overreaching. This can fairly be equated to the concept of Dharma.
  • We shall note that justice administered by state is a concept of impartiality, whereas the concept of justice as contemplated by Socrates is goodness, which is Dharma. In fact, Salmond Jurisprudence, 1948 Edition, mentions that the Justice is the good conduct, which promotes general well being of the community. This is quoted in Basu’s 6th Edition, Volume-A
  • So, the justice can be equated to Dharma. That is what our Supreme Court also recently said. Thus, the concept of Dharma is not religious concept. The word ‘Dharma’ or ‘Hindu Dharma’ denotes upholding, supporting, nourishing that which upholds, nourishes or supports the stability of the society, maintaining social order and general well-being and progress of mankind; whatever conduces to the fulfillment of these objects is Dharma, it is Hindu Dharma and ultimately ‘Sarva Dharma Samabhava’ (AIR SC ).
  • Now, we shall go to third principle of Socrates, the ruler should be philosopher.

Why the sovereignty has come on earth:

  • Maharshi mentioned, how the sovereignty has come into force. Devas requested God of Creation to remedy the situation which is unbearable on earth. They said, “An unrestrained sexual indulgence began. Men began to say what they liked. All distinctions between clean and unclean food and between virtue and vice disappeared.” (MB-12-59-20).
  • When enquired by the Lord of Creation why such conduct come into vogue, it was replied, “Ruler began to deny the authority of Vedas, tries to show that the scriptures should be disregarded and approve all breaches of control in society, that brought out the ruin” (MB-12-79-19). F
  • Hence, it was said, this ruling of kingdom is a state craft. Rajyam sumahattantram – ruling of the kingdom involves a great statecraft and can be achieved by only an accomplished person. It can never be protected by mere candour or simplicity (MB-12-58-21&22).
  • Maharsi gave extensive lessons of 365 chapters in Santi Parva. Raja dharma alone 75 chapters, Apad dharma, and Moksha dharma, which both ruler and the ruled must follow dharma.
  • So, in Raja Dharma, the following principles are embodied:
    • Raja Kalasya Karanam- he must be responsible for the time.
    • The Ruler should possess self control. Ajitatma narapatih vijayeta katham ripun – The king should first conquer himself and try to subdue his enemies be able to conquer his enemies.
    • He should not have any attachment towards wife, money and children – Dareshana, Dhaneshana, Putreshana.
      • We have got a tradition throughout the world, that if any calamity occurred to people in respect of a particular portfolio of a Minister, he should resign. As students we are not able to follow that. Our editors simply said it is a tradition in the world. Maharsi gave this axiom of Raja Kalasya Karanam and said that we should not doubt whether the king is responsible for time or not. He says he must have such a spiritual strength to change the time as he is responsible for every untoward event that takes place in his kingdom. There is no rain, he is responsible. There is excessive rain, he is responsible. There is an earthquake or an epidemic, he is responsible. We may think that this may relate to a different age. Maharsi says, it is not. He says all meritorious actions that people perform, the king automatically gets 1/4th of the benefit, provided he is sincere in securing benefit to the people without any self interest of his own. The principle is Yadadhite, Yaddhadati, Yadjuhoti, Yadarchati – of which they recite (like scriptures) or make a gift, or making a libation (yajna) or adoring a deity (MB-12-75-7). All that benefit is entitled, and hence there is no difficulty in securing spiritual strength for protecting the people.
  • This position can be secured provided he possesses self-control as stated in the second maxim and he should not have any attachment towards wife, money and children – Dareshana, Dhaneshana, Putreshana.
  • This is how we can spiritualise the democracy removing all its ills which can be secured only by practicing Dharma, both by people and also the Ruler. Thus, Dharma is a integrated principle for human society, which we have already noticed that the Lord has created Dharma as a rule of law and Dharma and the concept of Truth are same.
  • In this regard, we can divide the subject in these two Epics into three parts viz., Knowledge, Wisdom and Bliss. The other division is the study of the external world, internal world and the spirit. The scholars give another way of study like, physical science, moral science and spiritual science.
  • Now, we shall broadly deal with the first part of Knowledge relating to external world. It consists of:
    • Cosmology, Cosmogony, Cosmography (Chapter-1 & 2)
    • The concept of time (Chapter-3) – measure of time, play of time & quality of time
    • History of the Universe (Chapter-4)
    • Vidya (Chapter-13), 4 Vedas, 6 Vedangas, 4 Upangas, 4 Upavedas, 5 Darsanas.
    • Darsanas
    Cosmology, Cosmogony, Cosmography:
    The enquiry into self necessarily involves enquiry into creation. To know the creation, we must know the creator and your position in the Universe. We have noticed earlier that knowledge should embrace the physical science, moral science and spiritual science. These three topics are physical science. We can divide the subject of creation into four parts:
    • The creation of five elements and their roles in the universe;
    • The parts of the five elements in the body of beings, in particular human body;
    • The internal organs of human being and the spirit within;
    • The source of these five elements, which is the universal soul. The first and second parts constitute the physics. The third part is metaphysics – philosophy of the mind. The fourth is spiritual science or Cosmogony. Maharsi mentioned the five elements space, air, fire, water and earth and their subtle forms Sound, Touch, Form, Taste, Scent, respectively. He emphasized the importance of each element. Besides the subtle form of these elements, he has enumerated the qualities of these elements such as, earth has 9 forms of scent; water has six sorts of taste; the qualities of fire are 16; and the air 12 and the quality of sound is of 7 forms. Similarly, Maharsi gave the properties of these elements. Each element has 10 properties. Read the text.
  • The second part consisting of wisdom. It is virtually studying the internal world. I shall now mention the concept of Dharma. Maharsi mentioned 30 of them; Manu 10; Gautama 8; Taittriya Upanishad 25; Mahanarayana Upanisad 12; Rugveda 7. Maharsi highlighted all of them.
  • We have all noticed Dharmo Viswasya Jagatahprathista – Dharma supports the universe. It is the supreme means of liberation. It is significant to note, the Vedic Hymn that Dharma and Truth are same is highlighted by Maharsi stating that Truth is in 13 forms. They are, Samata – Impartiality, Dama - Self-control, Amatsaryam - Absence of malice, Ksama – Forgiveness, Hri - Modesty, Titiksa –Endurance, Anasuyata - Non-jealousy, Tyagah – Renunciation, Dhyanam – Meditation, Aryatvam Nobility, Dhrti – Fortitude, Daya – Compassion, Ahimsa - Non-injury. Thus, it is seen all of them are Dharma.
  • Sruti declares “Satyam Brahma” – Truth is God. It also declared – “Satyam Vada” – Speak truth. The word ‘Satya’ has got two meanings – Reality and Veracity. When we say Satyam Brahma it is reality. When we say Satyam Vada, it is veracity.
    • Maharsi declared the rules for a speech:
      • MB-12-299– 38. Silence is better than speech. To speak the truth is better than silence. To speak, again, truth what is connected with virtue is better than to speak the truth. To speak what, besides being true and righteous is agreeable, is better than to speak truth connected with virtue. There is another concept of Rta which means vow in a mind, the determination to speak the truth. Hence it is insincere is not truth Arjavam Brahmanapadam; all sorts of crookedness means death; so the utterance of truth should not comprise incorrect, insincere and dishonest statements. Stated positively it should be correct, sincere and honest statement.
    • He also prescribed the mode of speech. He gave a separate chapter for that : stating a good speech giving the celebrity.
      • Svantam Eka Padam – Purusah Samyagacaran - agreeableness speech makes a person an object of regard and acquires great celebrity. MB-12-84-3
    • Definition of truth :
      • A truth is that which highly beneficial to creatures.
  • It is said, Truth is the essence of veda; Self control is essence of truth; Sacrifice is the essence of self control; This is ever the conduct of the righteous. Thus, we have noticed, the concept of Truth and also the Dama, the Self-Control. Now, we must notice the Tyaga – Sacrifice, the essence of Dama.
  • The words Tyaga and Dana are used as synonymous words. Tyaga strictly connotes the idea of sacrifice or renunciation, while Dana is only an act of giving a thing or a property without consideration. Anusasana Parva is also named Dana Dharma Parva extensively narrating the efficacy of making several gifts and charities.
    • Sruti declared how the gift should be made and laid down six rules.
    • Sraddhaya Deyam : Give with dedication
    • Asraddhaya Deyam : Never offer with indifference
    • Sriya Deyam : Offering must be in accordance with your prosperity
    • Hriya Deyam : Offer with modesty
    • Bhiya Deyam : Offer with reverential fear
    • Samvida Deyam : Offer with friendly gesture
  • Maharsi also declared the following axioms highlighting charity:
    • Danam Ekapadam Yasah .. Charity gives highest fame
    • Danamasya Parayanam .. Charity is the chief resort for the mankind.
    • Danam Mitram Marisyatah .. Charity is the friend who is leaving this world
    • Daivam Danaphalam Proktam .. Fortune in this life is the result of gift in previous life
  • We shall now see the opposite concepts of ‘Kama’, ‘Krodha’ and ‘Lobha’ against which Maharasi warned, that they will degrade humanity. Kama basically means desire. It may relate to sexual desire or other desires of wealth, power and other desires for name and fame. If you fail to secure the desire, you will get anger. So, Dwija during his morning prayers regularly mentions “Kamo Karsi namo namah, Manyurakarsi namo namah”. He says, I am not responsible – the Kama is responsible. I salute that. I am not responsible – anger is responsible. Every human being succumbs to Kama and Krodha. The Epic Srimad Ramayanam mentions Sri Rama is the only person who never succumbed to Kama & Krodha.
  • The third concept is ‘Lobha’. Once we noticed Dharma is Jnana (Truth), Ajnana is Adharma (ignorance). From that we get delusion. From delusion to greed. Greed to desire. From desire to attachment. From attachment to hatred. The circle is complete. Ako lobha mahagraho, lobhat papam pravarthate (MB-12-158 & 163). Sruti declared in Isa Upanisad – whatsoever moves on earth should be covered by the Lord. Protect your Self through that detachment. Do not covet anybody’s wealth. Gandhiji very fond of this. There is enough wealth for all of us, but not sufficient to one greedy man.
  • I have also mentioned only concept of Ksama. Ksama Vasikrute Loke
  • We have also noticed the concept of administration of State. Both the Epics mentioned extensively the State Craft. Both as Raja Dharma and also Niti. We shall briefly notice that.
  • And also note what Churchill said.

    Ramayana and Mahabharata are two Itihasas – heroic histories of this country. In the West, these stories are classified as epics. We have also begun to state them as epics, after foreign rule, forgetting their character as Itihasas. The famous epics of the West are the Iliad and the Odyssey. An epic is defined as a poem narrating heroic legendary figures embodying a national concept of past history. The expression ‘legend’ indicates a notion of myth as a traditional story as historical, but unauthenticated. “The Wordsworth Encyclopedia” classified the Ramayana and Mahabharata as epics of India and stated that epics are “narrative poem or cycle of poems dealing with some great deed – often the founding of a nation or the forging of national unity – and often using religious or cosmological themes.” It is true that these two epics satisfy the requirement of ‘cosmological’, as they relate to earth and heaven, and human beings and angels mix together. Several episodes and events cannot be believed by common man hence they are termed as myths in the west. Epics instill greater values in humanity, and hence in the West they are found to be useful and essential for humanity. Even an iconoclast like Bernard Shaw acknowledged the essentialities of myths. In this country we know when the absolute descends on earth and takes a form; it will have powers and super natural qualities. We have such experiences of several incarnations. No Indian doubts the events in these epics. Further, sages Valmiki and Veda Vyasa are the contemporaries of the heroes in these stories and actually witnessed all the events. Hence these epics are live stories in this country and therefore they are well known Itihasas and not myths.

    These epics are not merely cosmological but also religious. The religious character of the epic, does not take away its national character. If all citizens belong to one religion, it constitutes the founding of a nation. If they belong to different religions, it forges national unity.

    The heroes of our epics Sri Rama and Yudhisthira are acclaimed and adored by people as “Vigrahavan Dharma”- personification of Dharma, and “Punya sloka”- whose name is auspicious”, respectively. Any Indian who reads these books imbibes several qualities of dharma – such as kshama – forgiveness, dama – self control, tyaga – sacrifice.

    These two epics exhort humanity to follow dharma, which is a duty oriented concept that involves sacrifice of self interest on the part of the individual. Now-a-days we see conflict of interests inside the country and outside the country and throughout the globe. Every one is conscious of rights and quite oblivious of their duties. State should promote Dharma to enable citizens to practice the same to achieve ideal manhood and civilization founded on dharma.

    Annie Besant said, ‘Dharma’ is a term which has now become part of the world vocabulary and dharma is India’s word for the world and India has a duty of spreading the idea of Dharma.’ She also said, in her work Sri Ramachandra, referring to these two epics, “Despite the influence wielded by these two books, however, their moulding power on life is not so great as once it was. If we could bring back their influence on character, we should indeed lift our India upwards.”

    On this concept of Dharma, Sister Nivedita said “Dharma can in no sense be taken as the name of a religion. It is essential quality, the permanent, unfluctuating core of substance - the man-ness of man, and life-ness of life, as it were.”

Thus, these two Epics undoubtedly promote Integration of the Nation.

Relevance Of Ramayana In Uplifting The Modern Man

-- Justice P. Kodanda Ramayya

I divide this topic into five parts viz., Age of Ramayana, its Message, its Source, Commentary thereon, Relevancy – both social order and democracy.

AGE :

It is well known that Ramayana is a very ancient epic in this country. Everyone knows the same. Its impact is green in the minds of humanity in spite of its origin beyond history. It is necessary to give its age, so that we could know its glory and its impact on humanity and its perennial message to humanity. In our country, our ancestors studied both astrology and astronomy and a clear measure of time of all events is recorded by them.

Almanac prepared in the earlier periods used to state as 1,25,69,013 as the Ravanabdas and 1,25,58,031 as Sri Rama’s Rajayantabadas (years) if we deduct the later figure from the earlier figure of Ravanabdas’s it comes 11,000 years which was the total number of years of Sri Rama’s rule and that clearly synchronizes with the period mentioned by the poet Valmiki in this epic. In Bramhanda Purana, it was mentioned that Sri Rama was born in Tretayuga on 24th Maha yuga. If we calculate as per that data we must exclude the Krutayuga of 24th Maha yuga and entire Treta yuga except 11,000 years (as Sri Rama was said to have been born at the end of Treta yuga after the sage Parasurama repetedly killed kshatriya race of the world) add the years of all the four yugas in 25th, 26th 27th and the present 28th Maha yuga upto this period which comes to 1,81,60,100 years by 1999 AD.

Kota Venkatachalam, a Vedic Scholar in his work Brahmanda Sristi Vijnanam in Telugu quoted a great Scholar Parvatam Nrusimha Deekshitulu as stating that the incarnation of Rama is of 1,46,16,000 years. In that reference, he has given other incarnations also. Hence, Sri Venkatachalam remarked that the statement in almanacs may require study as some error appears to have been crept in them. Further, Mulukutla Nrusimha Avadhani in his Ramayana Sara Sidhantham mentioned useful information regarding this.

It is true, scholars and investigators may differ on this question. However, this data is not meaningless. In a clear and methodical way our ancestors maintained this record and hence, it is incumbent on the part of this Viswa Sahitya Sanskriti Samsthan, New Delhi to make a thorough research and secure an authentic data through scholars.

MESSAGE:

Regarding message, Ramayana specifically mentions three concepts of Kama, Artha and extensively the subject of Dharma[ Kamartha Guna Samyuktam; Dharmardha Guna Vistaram (1-3-8 – Valmiki Ramayana) ]. Hence, the poet also said, Dharma follows wealth, from Dharma comes happiness, by recourse to Dharma one gets everything. Dharma is the essence of this world[ Aranya Kanda, Ch.9-30.] The concepts of Dharma and Satya are interchangeable and hence, Sage Valmiki extensively portrayed the character of Sri Rama as Satya Dharma Parayana - who practised Truth and Dharma with abiding faith. Though Ramayana did not mention the metaphysics or the concept of Moksha, the practise of these concepts of Dharma, Artha and Kama within the bounds of Dharma leads to redemption. Poet Valmiki built a great mansion of seven stories in the form of this epic. Truth and Dharma are the two pillars of base.

Fillial devotion and matrimonial purity are the two towers of this mansion. The poet installed Sri Rama in this mansion who is ever present whenever we open this book. Sri Rama practised several forms of Dharma which have become virtues making him Lokottara Purusa.

SOURCE:

Sage Valmiki himself said that he aims at elucidating the meaning of Vedas[ Vedartha Brmhanarthaya – 1-4-6- Valmiki Ramayana]. In fact, Veda Vyasa (herein after called Maharsi) said, Veda is transformed into Ramayana when the Lord visualised by Veda descended on earth as Sri Rama[ Vedavedye Parepumsi Jate Dasaradhatmaje, Vedah Praceta Sadasit Sakshat Ramayanatmana – Skanda Purana.]. It is well known that Taittiriya Upanisad 1-11 mentions 25 Dharmas starting with Satyam Vada, Dharmam Cara. Similarly, Maha Narayana Upanisad mentions the concept of Truth, Tapas, Dama (self-control), Sama (control of mind), Dana, Dharma, Performance of Sacrifices and other means of liberation. Further, the Vedic Hymns mentions several concepts both for social order and for redemption. It mentions Ayakshma sumana bhava – Free yourself from disease and cultivate good mindedness towards others (Taittiriya Samhita 4-5-1). Similarly, Mitrena cakshusapasya (Sukla Yajurveda 36-18) – See the whole world with friendly eyes. Rgveda also mentions seven maryadas – sins to be avoided viz., Brahma Hatya (killing a Vedic Scholar), Steya (stealing), Anyatalpa (Another’s bed - adultery), Anrta (telling a falsehood), Surapana (drinking alcohol), minor sins such as, refusing admission for a guest, neglecting Pitru Karyas, insulting elders. We cannot exhaust the several concepts mentioned in Veda and how they are embodied in this epic. In fact, Manu said, Vedokhilo Dharma Mulam.

In this connection it is useful to note what Veda Vyasa said about the content of Veda. He said, Vedas mention ten things for liberation of soul[ Maha Bharata, Santhi Parva, Ch.232-27]. They are, study of Vedas, adoption of domestic mode of life, penance, observation of all duties, common to all the modes of life, sacrifice, performance of all acts leading to pure fame, meditation which is of three kinds, and that kind of liberation called Siddhi attainable in this life. This definition is comprehensive embodying both Pravruthi and Nivruthi Dharma, concepts enunciated by Sage Veda Vyasa. Apparently, Ramayana mentions Pravruthi Dharma only, though it leads to Nivruthi Dharma. Hence, I take this opportunity to state that in every conference conducted by this Institution should study to what extent the Vedic Hymns pertaining to several topics of history, geography and science and other subjects and also other facets of Dharma are embodied in this Epic. It requires a constant and vigilant study as Vedic Hymns are infinite i.e., Ananta.

COMMENTARY:

Fortunately, like Greeks, we have got two Epics. The second Epic is Maha Bharata. It also aims at disseminating the knowledge of Dharma extensively both Pravruthi and Nivruthi Dharmas. Thus, it constitutes commentary on the concepts and precepts embodied in Ramayana.

In this connection, it is useful to compare these two epics and we will have the great advantage of studying these concepts of Dharma mentioned extensively in both these Epics. If Ramayana mentions an ancient story relating to moral or spiritual values, Maharsi crystalised and stated them as rules. In Ramayana the story relating to Somada, Sage Poulasya, Sage Kasyapa begetting children is stated by Maharsi thus. He said in different ages, birth took place by mere will, by look or by touch. The intercourse of the couple came later. Similarly, sage Valmiki gave the names of ten sage women such as Savitri and Damayanti. Maharsi gave the stories of all those meritorious women and extensively the story of Savitri and Damayanti. In Yudha Kanda, when Vibhisana sought asylum, Sugriva and other warriors warned Sri Rama not to accept him, as he was the brother of the enemy Ravana. Sri Rama just mentioned, “Have you not heard the story of the pigeon”. The story of pigeon is a very ancient story, long before Ramayana, that embodies the compassion and hospitality. Maharsi gave extensively eight Chapters about that story and he further said how the pigeons redeemed themselves and reached higher regions of heaven. Thus, it is said, Ramayana is Laksya Grandha, whereas Mahabharata is a Laksana Grandha. Maharsi gave the science of dharma for us. He defined what is dharma, why we should we practice dharma, what are the precautions to be taken, what is the advantage of practising it, what are the obstacles for practising it, what are the means of practising it. He fully explained the theory of dharma in several aspects. He defined as many as 30 dharmas. He began with the Vedic Hymn “Dharmo visvasya jagatah pratistha”[ Mahanarayana Upanisad, - 79 Anuvaka] dharma is the support of the whole universe. He amplified it saying the rules of Dharma are sanctioned for the conduct of the affairs of the world[ Lokayatrarthameveha dharmasya niyamah krtah – Santi Parva Chapter 259-4], for keeping the world together[ Lokasamgraha samyuktam – Santi Parva 259-26], for promoting advancement and growth of all beings[ Prabhavarthaya bhutanam dharma pravacanam – Santi Parva 109-10], for maintaining and upholding all beings[ Yatsyat dharanasamyuktam sa dharmahiti niscayah – Santi Parva 109-10] and for securing non-injury for all beings[ Ahimsarthaya bhutanam dharma pravacanam krtam – Santi Parva 109-11]. He also said that anything that is delightfully agreeable is Dharma[ Sarvam Priyabhyupagatam – Santi Parva 259-25] and the rules of Dharma are ancient, universal, friendly, beneficial and eternal[ Sanatanam, Sarvabhutahitam, Maitram, Puranam – Santi Parva 262-10].

The literal meaning of dharma is Dharati Lokaniti Dharmah – Dharma bears the world; Dhryate va Janairiti Dharmah – people bear Dharma. It is a rule of conduct enjoined to every human being as a duty.

Maharsi, having stated the several facets of Dharma, still gave all embracing working rules for humanity thus[ Anusasana parva 22-25]: Maryadayam sthito Dharmah: Keeping oneself within bounds is Dharma and Samascaivasya Laksanam – Self-control is its essence. Secondly, he summed up the theory of dharma in practice thus: “That which is antagonistic to one’s own self should never be done in respect of another. Briefly this is dharma”[ Na tat Parasya Sandadhyat Pratikulam yadatmanah – Mahabharata Udyogaparva 39-72 ].

Further, Ramayana expounded the necessity of a king and also the state craft in Ch.67 and 100 of Ayodhya Kanda. Maharsi extensively explained these concepts in Santi Parva under the Chapters of Raja Dharma and Apadharma. Sri Rama practised all these Dharmas and showed ever lasting path to the humanity to follow his practice of these dharmas.

In view of the fact that this Maha Bharata constitutes commentary on Ramayana, this Institution should take every opportunity to study and see how Maharsi highlighted the concepts mentioned in Ramayana by Sage Valmiki.

RELEVANCY:

I have divided the relevancy in two parts viz., for social order and for the democracy.

The modern man is under a mistaken impression that the concept of Dharma is a religious concept and he says that in the set up of democracy we require equality, liberty and fraternity, the political norms. It is equally a mistake to think that the three doctrines embodied in the Constitution of our country and other countries throughout the world are political doctrines. In fact, the three concepts are God Heads of Soul and as observed by Aurobindo. He observed,

“Freedom, equality, brotherhood are three godheads of the soul; they cannot be really achieved through the external machinery of society or by a man so long as he lives only in the individual and the communal ego. When the ego claims liberty, it arrives at competitive individualism. When it asserts equality, it arrives first at strife, then at an attempt to ignore the variations of Nature, and, as the sole way of doing that successfully, it constructs an artificial and machine-made society. A society that pursues liberty as its ideal is unable to achieve equality; a society that aims at equality will be obliged to sacrifice liberty. For the ego to speak of fraternity is for it to speak of something contrary to its nature”.

Hence he said, the brotherhood is the real key to the triple gospel of the idea of humanity. We cannot achieve these objects embodied in the Constitution as they are really virtues – different facets of dharma. Inspite of loud proclamation of these precepts, humanity has not really won them. Is it possible to practice precept of fraternity when persons born to the same parents look with reservation, suspicion, hatred and kill each other for property? Fraternity is not even claimed to be an enforceable principle. Equality can be enforced only against state but not against a co-citizen. The liberty we enjoy is really empty and unreal. Even married couple cannot move freely in the streets, let alone unmarried girls. Unless citizens follow Dharma and become virtuous, the state alone cannot enforce these doctrines and achieve the goal.

With reference to the concept of Social Order, two basic rules are enunciated by Sage Valmiki in his Epic; they are filial devotion and matrimonial purity. Sage Valmiki laid foundation for these values which is of perennial value. Sri Rama demonstrated it by his conduct and showed how the dutiful son should be. How a son should stand before a father, how he should approach him, how he should bow to him and heed to his request, adore him as god, unmindful of the consequences, is a great lesson given by the Sage. The Epic mentions the story of Sravanakumara, who reached higher regions because of devotion to his parents. Maharsi gave stories of filial devotion of Bhisma and Dharmavyadha, and also king Puru. He also said, the very best son is he who accomplishes what is wanted by his father without his request. A mediocre son is he, who does whatever his father commands, and lowest is he who refuses to obey the command of his father.

Coming to matrimonial purity, Swami Vivekananda said, “Sita is gone into the very vitals of our race. She is there in the blood of every Hindu man and woman. We are all children of Sita.” In Ramayana, there is an episode of test by fire. If it was not present, it would have only the story of a hero, full of valour like Greek epic story of Iliad, where Trojan war was waged by Greeks to bring back the wife of Menelaus, a beautiful lady Helen, when she was abducted by Paris, son of the king of Troy. The test by fire makes the epic, the epic of the east; gives purity, glory, lustre to the character of Sita Devi. When she was abandoned second time by Sri Rama due to public infamy, Sita Devi, while sending message to Sri Rama said, “Conduct yourself always among citizens, as you would do with your brothers. I do not feel penitent about my body. Do bear yourself in a manner through which the ill report among citizens is proved to be wrong. My husband is my deity. The task entrusted by my husband is dearer to me than my life.” Thus, it is seen, in practising dharma, Sita was nowhere inferior to her husband. Her mode of practice of dharma was love. If Sri Rama was Vigrahavan Dharmah – personification of Dharma, she is personification of love or compassion – devya karunya rupaya. Her anxiety to protect her husband from adharma is clear, lest he may show unwittingly any kind of anger towards citizens, who made imputations to her, though she was innocent. Sri Rama and Sita Devi have understood each other correctly, prepared to fulfil their respective duties in their lives as ideal couple and as an ideal kind and a queen.

It may appear to a modern man engaged in the present political atmosphere that these requirements of filial devotion and matrimonial purity are mere gospel of songs, neglecting social order. We are enjoying both these benefits by the blessing of sages without realising their importance. In the year 1942, on the hey day of Marxism in our country, a student of a University declared: “I need not respect my parents as I am their sixth son”. He meant that his parents were lustful not observing control of birth in the family. Is a son born, nurtured, brought up and educated by parents entitled to say so? Anyhow, we have reached such standards. This is apart from the mounting cases of maintenance in Civil Courts by parents against their children. We see, a rich nation like U.S.A. is being divided into two parts – old people and new. The new generation began to complain that all benefits are being given to the old people. All benefits would go to them and they are being neglected. Who are those old people? They are the parents of the new generation. We also saw some nations killing old people. There are instances of foreigners admiring the filial devotion shown in this country towards parents. It is the result of the deep impact made by Sri Rama on the people of this land.

Similarly, regarding matrimonial purity, Annie Besant said, “I found nowhere spirituality so pure and lofty, devotion so strong and tender, purity so unsullied and spotless, patience so wise and beautiful as are found even in the modern women of India. This is the great tradition that our country has achieved in our womanhood”. Day in and day out, we see along with cartoons, huge articles of injustice to women also popularly called gender justice. Once the matter was carried to the Supreme Court, when co-workers insulted a woman and the court imposed fines, the comments being the fine was too low. On the other hand, nobody ever said, “how could this happen in this country? Why should it happen?” Jesus Christ said, “you should not touch other man’s wife”, and also said that “adultery is an offence according to our law. Even looking at a woman with an adulterous eye is adultery”. We discarded dharma. Nobody is prepared to discuss about that, but simply repeat and fill columns with sensational news. Here is Sri Rama, about whom Valmiki said Naramah Paradharamscha caksurbhyamapi pasyati[ Chapter 2-72-48, Valmiki Ramayana.] - Sri Rama never looked at other man’s wife. His brother Laksmana, further surpassed him Though he was serving both Sri Rama and Sita Devi, he never looked at her except her feet. This is vividly described by Sage Valmiki in the epic twice. Further, Sri Rama also declared that the mother and motherland are superior to heaven – Janani Janma Bhumischa, Swargadapi Gariyasi. Thus, in the devotion to the parents, the dedication to kingdom and the sacredness of the marital life, in all the three, Sri Rama surpassed all the three worlds. In the epic story Ramayana, as Aurobindo said, Sage Valmiki portrayed an ideal manhood, a divine beauty of virtue, an ethical order, a civilisation founded on Dharma.

With reference to democracy, it is relevant to note what Socrates said and we will know the importance of this Epic and the practice of Dharma. He enunciated three basic principles of democracy for establishing an ideal State. The first is dividing the society into three classes of citizens, rulers, auxiliaries, and craftsmen. The second principle is citizens should practice the four cardinal principles of virtue – wisdom, courage, discipline and justice. The third principle is the ruler should be a philosopher. So far the classification of society is concerned, it appears it was not put into practice in any of the cities in Greece. But the idea behind the rule is that everyone must be prepared to sacrifice.

No one should have any attachment to their family and each one should take only one job, so that atleast rulers will have impersonal attitude while ruling the country. It is clearly duty oriented concept. The four cardinal principles of wisdom, courage, self-control and justice said to be pillars for democracy are extensively explained by Valmiki and Sri Rama practiced them showing path to the humanity. Valmiki gave eight characteristics of a sound and ripe intellect. They are

  • the desire to hear,
  • hearing,
  • receptivity,
  • retentive power,
  • reason,
  • against proposition,
  • faculty of apprehension, and
  • final realisation[ Valmiki Ramayana, 6-113-26]
It includes knowledge, experience, and ability to act.

With reference to courage, Sage Valmiki gave different facets of courage. Dhrti is the Sanskrit word for courage. Sage Valmiki gave six facets for this. They are Sattvam, Dhrti, Sthairyam, Viryam, Pratapa and Sauryam. They bear different meanings is indisputable, as Sage gave in one sloka all these possessed by the hero Sri Rama[ Valmiki Ramayana, 6-15-3].

The concept of self-control is called Dama by our Sages. Dama is controlling external organs. The Epic glorifies the conduct of Rama of his exercise of the concept of Dama. Sri Rama took vow to lead the life of a anchorite during his exile for fourteen years. Hence he took roots and fruits, observed strict adherence to that vow. The sages who received him also gave him the same food. When Guha offered him delicious dishes, he refused to take them saying that he was on vow with reference to food. Similarly, he observed celibacy during the days of exile and agreed to Sita Devi accompanying him after she promised him to observe celibacy during exile. Maharsi said, the characteristics and marks of self-controlled man are forgiveness, patience, abstention from injury, impartiality, truth, sincerity, control of sense, cleverness, mildness, modesty, firmness, liberality, freedom from anger, contentment, sweetness of words, benevolence, freedom from malice. All these combined make up self-control. Finally, Maharsi said that self-control is the door to immortality. Damah Dwaramhi Amrutasreha Vedmi[ S.C-299-20]. The highest among Dharmas is self-control. Self-control increases energy.

So far the fourth virtue of justice is concerned, Plato finally summed up as a virtue, which, enables him to practice other virtues and also, described it at the end as goodness. We shall note that justice administered by state is a concept of impartiality, whereas the concept of justice to be practised by the citizens is good conduct. Every man acts according to his self interest, but his act or conduct is said to be just only if it promotes the general well being of the community. Goodness mentioned by Socrates is Dharma. Maharsi extensively explained this concept in his work naming it as Dharma. However, it is enough if we note what he finally said about Dharma in practice: That which is antagonistic to one’s own self, should never be done in respect of another. Briefly this is Dharma.

Thus, the four principles enunciated by Socrates are extensively practised by Sri Rama as stated by Valmiki in his Epic. He said, Rama is virtue incarnate, pious and of unfailing prowess[ Aranya Kanda, Ch.37-13]. Sri Rama conquered men by his virtue; Vedic Scholars through charity; the elders through service and his enemies on the battle field with his bow[ Ayodhya Kanda, Ch.12-29]. Truthfulness, charity, asceticism, self-abnegation, goodwill, purity, straightforwardness, learning, service to elders, these are constantly present in him[ Ayodhya Kanda, Ch.12-30]. Sri Rama himself declared his abiding faith on Dharma and said to his mother Kaika, “Commanded by you, I would myself joyfully part with, in favour of Bharata, not only the kingdom of Ayodhya and my personal property, but even Sita my wedded wife, nay my beloved life itself[ Ayodhya Kanda, Ch.19-7]. He also said, “I do not desire to live in the world as a slave to material gains; know me to be devoted to immaculate righteousness like Rishis[ Ayodhya Kanda, Ch.19-20]. Consoling brother Laksmana for the calamity of exile, he said, “O Laksmana, do not give way to remorse over the loss of fortune. To me, it matters little. Whether sovergnity falls to my lot or exile, the latter being of greater consequence (in that it will be free from worries and favourable for ascetism and afford me opportunity to redeem father’s pledge given to Kaikeyi)[ Ayodhya Kanda, Ch.22-29].

With reference to the philosopher king as envisaged by Socrates, what is stated above clearly brings out the requirement ruler being the philosopher. Both the epics Ramayana and Mahabharata mentioned the list of philosopher kings known as Rajarsis. They were kings, but they were sages. We have notice already that Sri Rama declared Viddhimam rsibhih Tulyam Kevalam Dharma masthitam[ Valmiki Ramayana 2-19-20 ] - Know me to be devoted to immaculate righteousness like a Rsi. and also the poet describing him Ramo Vigrahavan Dharmah – Rama is the personification of righteousness. Maharsi gave two axioms about a ruler. He said Ajitatma narapatih vijayata katham ripun[ S.C.69-4] “The king should first conquer himself and try to subdue his enemies. How can a king who has not been able to conquer his own self, be able to conquer his enemies”. The other axiom being Raja Kalasya Karanam[ S.C.79-69] - Virtually king makes the age. Maharsi says, “you should not doubt whether it is the king that makes the age or it is the age that makes the king. The truth is king makes the age”. Thus, for every event, king takes responsibility.

A minister for Railways resigns for an accident that took place on a railroad, though he is innocent for that. But the rule is he must have such moral responsibility that in his administration, no untoward incident should occur and he should take responsibility for that. This is virtually the spiritual law, with all humility the administrator should follow. This is the great tradition that is embodied in this axiom.

Socrates says that the ruling class should have no family life so that he should not be attached to the members of the family. That is exactly the same principle that these epics say without denying the family life to the king. The principle embodied in the concepts Daresana, Dhanesana, Putresana – attachment towards wife, attachment towards money and attachment towards a son respectively must be conquered by the king. We have got a catena of rulers given in the epic, known as Rajarsis, who ruled the country in such an impersonal way without any attachment to wife, money or children. Sri Rama practiced Dharma and he secured innumerable good qualities, both general virtues and also virtues required to a ruler.

Thus we see that the Ruler should be the Philosopher and the four virtues said to be foundation for democracy are clearly found in this epic for all human beings, to follow to make their lives successful and also become ideal members of the society.

In this connection, it is necessary to remember that the concept of Dharma is not a mere philosophical attitude in life abandoning all action even ignoring the evil. On the other hand, the epic Ramayana enjoins that for the protection of the people even that which is sinful or wrong must invariably be done by the Ruler whose duty is to protect the people 30. Maharshi Veda Vyasa evolved from this, the principle of Niskama Dharma making the Ruler to perform his duty desireless and without any enemity.

Sri Aurobindo observed, “Modern India since Musulman advent has accepted the politics of Canakya in preference to Vyasa’s – deep preoccupation with ethical issues of speech and action is very notable”. He further said, after the disappearance of Ksatriya caste, Brahmins were only interpreting the Bhagavad Gita, and dealt only on the element of quiescence in it. But they have not echoed Sri Krishna’s emphasis on the necessity of action as the one sure road to the goal.” He also said, “Christian and Buddhism doctrine of turning the other cheek to the smiter, is dangerous as it is impracticable and mankind has never been either able or willing to carry into practice”. Sri Aurobindo readily drew the illustration for the above principle from these two epics and in particular how Sri Rama acted. He said, “Rama’s duty as a son calls him to sacrifice himself, to leave the empire of the world and become a beggar and a hermit; he does it cheerfully and unflinchingly; but when Sita is taken from him, it is his duty as a husband to rescue her from her ravisher and as a Kstriya to put Ravana to death if he persists in his wrong-doing. This duty also he pursues with the same unflinching energy as the first. He does not shrink from the path of the right because it coincides with the path of self-interest. ………… When Ravana came for the first time to the battlefield, Sri Rama crushed his vanity and joy by breaking his bow into pieces, killing his horses and the charioteer, breaking his crown into pieces and made Ravana swoon down for some time in a state of helplessness. Thereupon Sri Rama said: You have performed a great and exceedingly terrible feat, and you are fully exhausted. You go back to your house, take rest, and come back with a new chariot and weapons afresh. Then I will show my strenght”31.

This is the best illustration of Niskama Dharma.

A compassion shown to an enemy even during war. Ravana was one who abducted his wife and refused to give back and was waging war to retain her permanently. Would it be possible for an ordinary man to show compassion for such an enemy? This is why Laksmana described Sri Rama “Ripunamapi Vatsala” – He is benevolent even with the enemy.

Even after the death of Ravana, we have yet another instance which speaks volumes of Rama’s virtue of practising the Dharma and the absence of enmity. When Vibisana was reluctant to perform cremation ceremony of Ravana after his death, Sri Rama disapproved such attitude and said: “hostility should cease with death.”

Ravana is mine as well as yours. He is a mighty warrior, richly deserves due ceremonies in accordance with the prescribed code of conduct. An ordinary man would think of throwing the body of Ravana into sea and that would finish the matter. But Sri Rama was not of that type. He is Satpurusa. He is the foremost among men. Hence, the divine Sage Narada described him as ‘Sadhu. Sri Rama said to Vibhisana, “Your brother is my brother, because, with his death, the enmity ceases” and directed him to perform due ceremonies with all reverence.

Thus, we see, the epic Ramayana is not a mere Kavya aiming at giving pleasure to the humanity. But is very instructive. That is why, Sri Aurobindo said that Homer and Shakespeare greatest poets in the world, our epics Ramayana and Maha Bharata are built on an almost cosmic vastness of plan and take all human life and human thought as well in their scope and touch on things which Greek and Elizabethan poets could not even glimpse. Hence, he said, “These epics are therefore not a mere mass of untransmutted legend and folklore, as is ignorantly objected, but a highly artistic representation of intimate significances of life, the living presentment of a strong and noble thinking, a developed ethical and aesthetic mind and a high social and political ideal, the ensouled image of a great culture. As rich in freshness of life but immeasurably more profound and evolved in thought and substance than the Greek, as advanced in maturity of culture but more vigorous and vital and young in strength than the Latin epic poetry, the Indian epic poems were fashioned to serve a greater and complete national and cultural function and that they should have been received and absorbed by both the high and the low, the cultured and the masses and remained through twenty centuries and intimate and formative part of the life of the whole nation is of itself the strongest possible evidence of the greatness and fineness of this ancient Indian culture.

As we are considering the relevancy of the epic to the modern man, I conclude with this statement of Annie Besant referring these two epics in her work “Sri Ramachandra”, “Despite the infuence wielded by these two books, however, their moulding power on life is not so great as once it was. If we could bring back their influence on character, we should indeed lift our India upwards”.

Collateral points glorifying the Epics and also the concept of Dharma, which is not a religion:

  • Ramayana and Mahabharata are two Itihasas – heroic histories of this country. In the West, these stories are classified as epics. We have also begun to state them as epics, after foreign rule, forgetting their character as Itihasas. The famous epics of the West are the Iliad and the Odyssey. An epic is defined as a poem narrating heroic legendary figures embodying a national concept of past history. The expression ‘legend’ indicates a notion of myth as a traditional story as historical, but unauthenticated.
  • “The Wordsworth Encyclopedia” classified the Ramayana and Mahabharata as epics of India and stated that epics are “narrative poem or cycle of poems dealing with some great deed – often the founding of a nation or the forging of national unity – and often using religious or cosmological themes.”
  • It is true that these two epics satisfy the requirement of ‘cosmological’, as they relate to earth and heaven, and human beings and angels mix together. Several episodes and events cannot be believed by common man hence they are termed as myths in the west. Epics instill greater values in humanity, and hence in the West they are found to be useful and essential for humanity. Even an iconoclast like Bernard Shaw acknowledged the essentialities of myths. In this country we know when the absolute descends on earth and takes a form; it will have powers and super natural qualities. We have such experiences of several incarnations. No Indian doubts the events in these epics. Further, sages Valmiki and Veda Vyasa are the contemporaries of the heroes in these stories and actually witnessed all the events. Hence these epics are live stories in this country and therefore they are well known Itihasas and not myths.
  • You all remember, the incident of Union of India filing an affidavit in ‘Sethu’ case in the Apex Court, that there is no proof that Rama was born. This is the result as we have forgotten the concept that these historic stories of Ramayana and Maha Bharata are Itihas, but not myths.
  • Sri Aurobindo classified world’s greatest poets – in first row – Homer, Shakespeare, and Valmiki; in second row Dante and Kalidas and observed “When I said there were no greater poets than Homer and Shakespeare, I was thinking of their essential force and beauty – not of the scope of their work as a whole; for there are poets greater in their range. The Mahabharata is from that point of view a far greater creation than the Iliad, the Ramayana than the Odyssey, and spread, either and both of them, their strength and their achievement over a larger field than the whole dramatic world of Shakespeare; both are built on an almost cosmic vastness of plan and take all human life (the Mahabharata all human thought as well) in their scope and touch too on things which the Greek and Elizabethan poets could not even glimpse”. That is the glory and efficacy of these two epics.
  • Annie Besant said, ‘Dharma’ is a term which has now become part of the world vocabulary and dharma is India’s word for the world and India has a duty of spreading the idea of Dharma.’ She also said, in her work Sri Ramachandra, referring to these two epics, “Despite the influence wielded by these two books, however, their moulding power on life is not so great as once it was. If we could bring back their influence on character, we should indeed lift our India upwards.”
  • On this concept of Dharma, Sister Nivedita said “Dharma can in no sense be taken as the name of a religion. It is essential quality, the permanent, unfluctuating core of substance - the man-ness of man, and life-ness of life, as it were.”
  • Banning Richardson, M.A., (Hons.) (Cantab), A.B. (Princeton) in his work East and West Meet in Maharshi (Ramana Maharshi) observed at page-23 (Golden Jubilee Souvenir – 1896 – 1946) as under:

    “Aristotle may be termed the first scientist of Western civilization. His conception of spirit was, like that of the nineteenth century evolutionists, that of an attribute which emerges out of the primeval slime. So, when medieval European scholars turned away from the truly spiritual tradition of Plato and his Alexandrian follower Plotinus to the scientific but fundamentally anti-spiritual Aristotle, they sacrificed the basis of their civilization along with a great deal of obscurantism which admittedly had to go.

    With the coming of the Renaissance the next inevitable step was taken, - science was separated from religion, which meant that it became its own master, uncontrolled by spiritual direction. Finally, in the eighteenth century, which may be said to be the last peak of Christian civilization, religion was separated from philosophy and the devastation was complete.”

  • Thus, we see there is no conflict between religion and science in our country. Similarly, there is no conflict between philosophy and religion.
  • As Swami Ranganathananda said, the word ‘dharma’ stands for integrating principle in human society. Next to truth, Dharma is the most significant concept in Indian culture.
  • Maharsi said in Chapter 110 “Those, who bow to all gods, who listen to the doctrines of all creeds, who have faith, who are possessed of tranquil souls succeed in getting over all difficulties.”
  • Poet Tagore remarked that epithets of disparaging nature find an easy target in India bespattering her skin and piercing her vital parts. Epithets once given in circulation diehard. Hence, the duty on the part of every citizen is to promote integrity and prosperity of the nation.
  • He also remarked, we could never do justice from a mere sense of duty to those for whom one lacks respect. So each religion should respect the other as each religion has its own spiritual heights. One in Truth, another in compassion, another Love and yet another in Equality. Hence we should never try to destroy another as Sages said we should try to fulfill not to destroy.
  • Sri Vinobha Bhave declared that “after reading Holy Quran my Hindutva was washed away”.
  • Swamy Vivekananda said Emperor Akbar was practically a Hindu.
  • In ‘Constitution of the World’, the author M.V. Pyle, says “there is a practice in England. He stated thus: “Religious education has to be provided in all schools financed from public funds and is part of the national curriculum. Parents have the right to ask for their children to be withdrawn from such classes.”
  • Swamy Vivekananda said, if you want to be a true reformer, three things must be necessary.
    • Are you full of ideas of sympathy?
    • Have you discovered the means by which to keep the gold alone without dross?
    • What is your motive? Are you sure that you are not actuated by greed of gold, by thirst of fame or power? (Page 158 – Vol.4 – Complete works of Vivekananda (Mayawati Memorial Edition)).

RAJADHARMA IN RAMAYANA

“In a land destitute of a ruler the thundering cloud wreathed with lightning does not drench the earth with rain water. (9) In a rulerless land handfuls of seeds are no longer scattered (for fear of uncertainty of crops). (Nay) in a rulerless land a son is not amenable to the control of his father nor is a wife amenable to the control of her husband (there being no executive authority to enforce correct conduct). (10) In a rulerless land there is no personal property (in the absence of a protector) nor is there a (faithful) wife in a rulerless land (there being no law to check adultery). There is this great risk in a rulerless territory. How can any other form of truthfulness (virtue) prevail? (11) In a rulerless land, people do not construct assembly halls (for public gatherings, there being no such gatherings) nor do joyous men plant lovely gardens (for fear of their being destroyed by enemies of peace and order) or build sacred houses (such as temples and buildings for the free accommodation of travelers and strangers etc.). (12) In a rulerless land the twice-born (other than Brahmans, viz, Ksatriyas and Vaisyas) given to the performance of sacrifices and self-controlled Brahmans observing austere vows do not undertake sacrificial performances in which everyone participating in it is both an officiating priest and a sacrificer. (13) In a rulerless land (even) Brahmans richly endowed with wealth do not pay handsome sacrificial fees to the priests officiating (even) at big sacrificial performances (wherever such performances are undertaken, lest they may be taken for rich men and looted by brigands). (14) In a rulerless land festivals (in honour of deities) in which actors and dancers exhibit their art in a highly ecstatic mood, and convivial gatherings promoting the welfare of the state do not gather strength. (15) In a rulerless land parties to a law-suit are not able to have their dispute settled, nor are those given to hearing stories from the Puranas etc. pleased with such stories told by those to whom the narration of such stories is agreeable. (16) In a rulerless land virgins decked with gold ornaments do not for their part go united to gardens to sport at dusk (for fear of being abducted or violated by miscreants). (17) In a rulerless land wealthy husband men and cowherds, even though well protected (by their attendants) do not sleep with open doors (for fear of burglars and dacoits). (18) “In a rulerless land lustful men do not drive in swift-going conveyances with women to pleasure-groves (for enjoying the forest scenery). (19) In a rulerless land sixty-year-old (army) tuskers do not (for fear of being deprived of their tusks or bells) move about on the main roads with bells fastened to their girths. (20) In a rulerless land the sound of plucking the bow-string with the palm produced by Ksatriyas uninterruptedly discharging arrows while practicing the use of bows is not heard. (21) In a rulerless land merchants traveling far and wide do not safely move about fully equipped with abundant salable goods. (22) In a rulerless land self-controlled ascetics moving all by themselves and contemplating on the Self with their own mind and taking up their abode wherever the evening falls do not move about (freely for want of hospitable householders). (23) In a rulerless land there is no acquisition of property and no security of possessions. Nor is the army able in a rulerless land to vanquish the foes in a battle. (24) In a rulerless land people do not quickly move out decked with ornaments in chariots driven by spirited horses of excellent breed. (25) In a rulerless land men well-versed in sacred lore do not meet (freely) holding disputations in forests and groves. (26) In a rulerless land flowers, sweetmeats and sacrificial fees are not got together for the worship of deities by self-controlled men. (27) In a rulerless land princes smeared with paste of sandal and aloe wood do not figure prominently as trees in the vernal season. (28) A state without a ruler is really no better than rivers without water, a woodland without grass and cows without a keeper.
