(29) A standard is the distinguishing mark of a chariot, while smoke is a testimony to (the presence of) fire. King Dasaratha, who (likewise) brought us, counselors, into prominence (by bestowing this honour on us) has passed from the human state to the state of gods. (30) In a rulerless land nothing is one’s own in the eyes of anyone. Like fishes men always devour one another. (31) (In the absence of a stable government) even those who do not believe in life after death and have flagrantly violated the rules of conduct prescribed by the Vedas and who have (consequently) been tormented with punishment inflicted by the king and whose fear (of punishment) has (now) been dispelled (by the anarchy prevailing at the time) are able to exercise authority (over others). (32) (Just) as the eye ever strives for the good of the body (by serving as a guide to it and showing it the right path), so does the king, who is the fountain of truth and righteousness, ever strive for the good of the state. (33) The king is truthfulness and virtue (incarnate); the king constitutes the nobility of birth in men of a high pedigree. The king is the mother as well as the father; the king is the benefactor of men. (34) (Even) Yama (the god of retribution), Kubera (son of Visrava, the god of riches), Indra (the ruler of gods) and the very mighty Varuna (the deity presiding over water) are oustripped by a king of excellent conduct by virtue of such conduct (inasmuch as he combines in himself the virtues of all the abovenamed deities). (35) If there is no king demarcating good and evil in the world, oh, world will be reduced to (utter) darkness as it were and nothing can be clearly perceived. (36) Even while the emperor was alive, we all never violated your command only any more than the oceans overstep the coastline on reaching it. (37) Therefore, fully considering our (past) conduct (which has ever been loyal to the king and devoted to the best interests of the state) and looking on the state, which has been converted into a (veritable) jungle for want of a king, crown you alone as king on this throne any prince who is a scion of Ikswaku or anyone else, O jewel among the Brahmans!

(10) Is your (personal) priest ( a son of Vasistha),-who is rich in humility, born of a noble pedigree, a man of extensive learning, free from spite and skilled in all pious and sacred rites, - treated with honour (by you)? (11) Does the priest employed by you to look after the sacred fires, who is well-versed in the procedure of conducting sacrificial performances, is endowed with intelligence and guileless of disposition, invariably inform you in time about a sacred fire having (already) been or going to be fed with oblations? (12) Do you hold in high esteem gods and manes, dependants, elders, kinsmen of your father’s age, the aged, the physicians as well as the Brahmans, my darling? (13) Do you respect Sudhanva, your teacher (of the science of archery), who is equipped with knowledge relating to the use of excellent arrows (discharged without uttering spells) and missiles (propelled with the utterance of spells) and well-versed in political economy? (14) I hope counselors who are valiant like you and full of learning, have controlled their senses, are born of a high pedigree and know the meaning of signs, have been secured by you, my darling (15) A secret well-guarded by ministers who are the foremost counselors and well-versed in politics is the root of victory of kings, O scion of Raghu ! (16) I hope you do not fall a prey to excess of sleep and wake betimes, And do you contemplate during the late hours of the night on the methods of acquiring wealth? (17) I hope you do not deliberate alone (without consulting anyone) nor do you take counsel with many. I hope the decision arrived at by you through deliberation (with your counselors) do not reach the public (before it is carried out. (18) I hope, considering your interest fully, you launch an enterprise betimes with a modest beginning but pregnant with great potentialities, and do not defer it, O scion of Raghuy? (19) I hope your vassals actually know of all your projects only when they have been duly carried out or at least well-nigh carried out and not so long as they are yet to be carried out. (20) I hope your deliberations which have not been proclaimed are not known (by others) through surmises or reasoning and the deliberations of others are known (before they materialize) by yourself or by your ministers. (21) I hope you prefer one learned man to thousands of fools inasmuch as a learned man is calculated to do immense good in financial crises. (22) If a king maintains even thousands or even tens of thousands of fools, no assistance can be expected from them (in times of need). (23) Even one minister who is talented, valiant, clever and well-versed in politics can bring a large fortune to a king or prince. (24) I hope high-class servants are entrusted by you with important duties, middling ones with second-rate affairs and third-grade servants with menial duties, my darling ! (25) I hope you entrust first-rate ministers, who are past (all) tests (loyalty), are hereditary servants of the crown and honest, with the highest duties. (26) I hope, sore agitated by stern rule the people in your state do not show disrespect to your ministers, O son of Kaikeyi? (27) I hope priests conducting sacrifices for the benefit of others do not disregard you (by refusing to officiate at your sacrifice) as they would a fallen man or (even) as matrons would despite their (own) husband who had taken to wife a lowborn woman and was (excessively) fond of her. (28) He who does not get rid of a physician adept in devices of aggravating a malady, a servant intent on bringing disgrace (to his master) and a gallant warrior seeking kingly power is (himself) killed (by these). (29) I hope a man who is (ever) pleased and full of resolution, gallant and talented, spotless (of character) and well-born, devoted and clever, has been appointed (by you) as the generalissimo? (30) Have the foremost of your heroic warriors, who are possessed of might and skilled in warfare, and whose prowess has been witnessed (on the field of battle), been treated with honour by you after being politely received? (31) I hope you distribute (daily) provisions and disburse the (monthly) salary due to them at the opportune time in a suitable manner and do not defer their payment. (32) Salaried servants surely and positively get enraged even at their master when the distribution of their provisions and the disbursal of their salary are delayed and that (itself) is a very great harm done (to the State). (33) I hope all the principal members of your race (the Ksatriyas) are devoted to you? (And) do they devoutly and cheerfully lay down their lives for your sake ? (34) Has a man of your own state, who is learned, clever, ready-witted and capable of delivering messages correctly, nay, who is able to distinguish between right and wrong been appointed by you as an ambassador, O Bharata? (35) Do you keep an eye on the eighteen functionaries of the enemies and the fifteen functionaries of your own side through three unknown spies told off for each ? (36) I hope you remain ever mindful of your enemies expelled (from their home by you) and since returned, even though they are (apparently) weak, O destroyer of foes ! (37) I hope you do not patronize atheistic Brahmans, O dear brother; for, ignorant and conceited as they are, they are skilled (only) in perverting the mind (and thereby diverting people from the righteous path). (38) Banking (solely) on their logical acumen, these men of perverted intellect preach nonsense (even) in the presence of foremost books on Dharma (piety). (39) I hope you protect on all sides the prosperous (city of) Ayodhya, rightly so called (because of its being unassailable), which has been occupied (in the past), O dear brother, by our heroic forbears, which is provided with strong gates and remains crowded with elephants, horses and chariots, which is inhabited in thousands by noble Brahmans (members of the priestly class), Ksatriyas (members of the warrior class) and Vysyas (members of the agriculturist and trading classes),- who are ever devoted to their (respective) duties, have subdued their senses and are full of great zeal,-nay, which is crowded with palatial buildings of various patterns and is thronged with learned men and full highly contended people. (40-42) I hope the kingdom (of Ayodhya),-which is overspread with hundreds of altars (constructed for sacrificial performances) and crowded with well-established men, which is adorned with temples, sheds where water is stocked for free distribution to passers-by and tanks, and peopled with highly delighted men and women, which is graced by social festivities and is full of well-cultivated fields and abounds in cattle, which is totally free from violence and does not depend (exclusively) on rains (for its agricultural products), which is charming (in point of topography) and is purged of beasts of prey, which is completely rid of all fears and is studded with mines, (nay) which is destitute of sinful men and was well-protected by our forbears, - is prosperous and faring well, O scion of Raghu ! (43-46) I hope the Vaisyas (who live by agriculture and rearing the bovine race) are all loved by you. Are these people, depending as they do on the vocation of a Vaisya (viz, trade, agriculture and breeding of cattle) thriving well at present, O dear brother? (47) I hope protection has been afforded by you to them by making available to them the objects desired by them and warding off evil; for all the inhabitants of one’s land deserve to be protected righteously by a king. (48) Do you keep your womenfolk pacified? Are they duly protected by you? I hope you do not repose excessive faith in them and do not confide your secrets to them (49) Are the forests which are the home of elephants preserved by you? Are milch cows in abundance with you? I hope you do not feel satisfied with the (existing) number of female elephants, horses and elephants (owned by you). (50) Getting up regularly every day do you show yourself well-adorned to the people in the assembly hall in the forenoon, O prince? (51) I hope all your workmen do not appear unhesitatingly before you nor are they kept (altogether) out of your sight. (In fact) a middle course is undoubtedly profitable in this behalf. (52) Are all your fortifications fully supplied with wealth and provisions, arms and water, mechanical contrivances and equipped with artisans and bowmen? (53) Is your income (sufficiently) large (to meet your expenses) and your expenditure comparatively less? I hope your wealth does not go to undeserving men, O scion of Raghu! (54) I (further) hope your wealth is expended (exclusively) in the cause of gods and manes as well as over the Brahmans and unexpected visitors, warriors and hosts of friends. (55) I hope no pious soul, who is pure of mind and sinless, is convicted from greed, when charged with some offence, though not found guilty by those well-versed in law-books. (56) I hope no thief who has been detected at the time of theft and apprehended on sufficient ground and also questioned (and found guilty) is released from greed of wealth, O jewel among men ! (57) In an unhappy contention between an affluent and a (financially) feeble man, O scion of Raghu, do your ministers of vast learning judge the case impartially? (58) The tears that drop from the eyes of men falsely convicted, O scion of Raghu, destroy the sons and cattle of a king who rules the people (merely) for the sake of pleasure (caring little for equity and justice). (59) Do you seek, O scion of Raghu, to win over the elders, children and foremost physicians by the following three means, viz., gifts, a loving mind and polite words? (60) Do you greet your teachers and elders, ascetics, deities and unexpected visitors as well as all the trees standing at cross roads (and believed to be the abode of gods) and Brahmans who have achieved the object of their life (through their learning, character and austerities)? (61) I hope you do not cause interruption in your religious practices by your (excessive) devotion to wealth (and devoting the morning hours and forenoon to the pursuit of wealth and other secular interests) nor do you mar your earthly interests by your over emphasis on religion (and allowing your religious duties to encroach upon the midday hours and afternoon reserved for the pursuit of wealth and other secular interests) nor do you mar both your religious and secular interests by your self-indulgence in the form of avidity for gratification of the senses. (62) Do you pursue all the (three) objects of human pursuit, viz., wealth and the delights of sense and religion (only during the hours allotted to each, O bestower of boons, dividing them according to time, O jewel among the victorious, knowing as you do the time appropriate for each? (63) Do the Brahmans that have thoroughly grasped the meaning of all the scriptures pray for your welfare along with the citizens (of Ayodhya) and the people of the countryside, O exceptionally wise prince? (64) Do you abjure the following fourteen failings of kings, viz., disbelief in the other world, mendacity (through greed etc.), anger, neglect of kingly duties, procrastination, shunning the wise, sloth, thralldom of the five (senses), devoting thought to the affairs of the State without seeking the advice of others (ministers etc.), taking counsel with those of perverted insight, failure to launch projects (already) decided upon, failure to keep secrets, failure to utter auspicious words at the beginning of every undertaking and rising from one’s seat (indiscriminately) to receive all (who happen to approach him)? (65-67) I hope you deal properly after knowing them in reality with the (well-known) ten evils (born of concupiscence), the five kinds of fortifications, the four expedients (recommended for kings), the seven (important) limbs of a state, the eight evils (born of anger) or the eight measures (conducive to the welfare of a state), the three (worldly) objects of human pursuit (viz, religious merit, material wealth and sensuous enjoyment) or the three kinds of power (viz, energy or …………, the power of dominion or……….. and the power of counsel or ……….), the three branches of learning (viz, the three Vedas or ….., the knowledge relating to agriculture, commerce and other vocational pursuits or …….and political science or ………) subjugation of the senses, the six strategic expedients (viz, coming to terms with the enemy- ………, waging war against him-…….., marching against him-…….. biding one’s time to seek a favourable opportunity-…………., causing dissension in the enemy’s ranks-……… and seeking the protection of a powerful ally-…….; adversity brought about by divine agencies (such as fire, water in the shape of excessive rains or floods, epidemic or endemic diseases, famine and pestilence) and by human agencies (such as officials, thieves, enemies, a king’s favourites and the king himself when actuated by greed; the (stern) duty of a king (as dictated by policy, such as to win over enemy’s men whose emoluments have been withheld, who are greedy or haughty, who have suffered indignity at his hands, who are irate or have been provoked by him for no reason, who are afraid or have been intimidated); (the following) twenty types of monarchs (who are not worth negotiating with, viz, 1. a king who is yet a child or 2. aged, 3. who has been ailing for a long time, 4. who has been ostracized by his own kith and kin, 5. who is pusillanimous or 6. is surrounded by cowards, 7.who is greedy or 8. has greedy associates, 9. who has estranged his ministers and others, 10. who is extremely voluptuous, 11. who confers with fickle-minded persons, 12. who speaks ill of divine beings and Brahmans, 13. who is ill-fated and 14. a fatalist, 15. who is afflicted by famine and 16. by military reverses, 17. who (mostly) remains away from home, 18. who has numerous enemies, 19. who is in the clutches of adverse times and 20. who is not devoted to truth and piety); the entire population (of the State); setting forth on an expedition (for conquest against an enemy); drawing up an army in battle-array; coming to terms with an enemy and waging war against him, the first of which serves as a ground for the two policies of duplicity and seeking the protection of a powerful enemy, while the other serves as a ground for the other two policies of marching against the enemy and biding time to seek a favourable opportunity to march against an enemy, O highly intelligent scion of Raghu ! (68-70) Do you hold consultation in accordance with scriptural injunctions with only four or three (selected) counselors collectively or severally (to guard against a split among them and to prevent the secrets from leaking out), O wise one? (71) Has your study of the Vedas borne fruit (in the shape of moulding your conduct according to their injunctions) ? Are your undertakings fruitful? Has your spouse given birth to offspring? Has your learning borne fruit (in the shape of culture and humility) ? (72) I hope your conclusion is precisely the same as mine, which has been set forth (in the foregoing verses), O scion of Raghu, and which is conducive to longevity and fame and is attended with religious merit, enjoyment and wealth. (73) Do you follow the way of life which our father treads and which our forbears trod, nay, which is in accord with the way of the virtuous and which is righteous (in itself) ? (74) I hope you do not partake by yourself of food nicely cooked. I hope you offer it to friends who seek it. (75) Having obtained (as his share), and ruled in the right way over, the entire globe, a wise Ksatriya holds sway over the earth and administering justice to the people quite in consonance with righteousness surely ascends to heaven which detached from this (mortal) body. (76)

DEVARSHI NARADA

Part played by Devarshi Narada

  • In Sri Valmiki Ramayanam.
  • Sri Veda Vyasa Mahabharatam
  • Sri Veda Vyasa Bhagavatam

Supplement to the Translation to the Introduction of the Message of Mahabharata

Ramayana and Mahabharata are two Itihasas – heroic histories of this country. In the West, these stories are known as epics. We have also begun to classify them as epics. It is well known that an epic is a narration of the life of heroic or legendary figures and implies that it is unauthenticated and hence a myth. Only few countries have these epics. The great epics of the West are the Iliad and the Odyssey. “The Wordsworth Encyclopedia” classified the Ramayana and Mahabharata as epics of India and stated that epics are “narrative poem or cycle of poems dealing with some great deed – often the founding of a nation or the forging of national unity – and often using religious or cosmological themes.” It is significant to note that the National character of the epic is not lost, simply because it is a religious story or a theme of a particular religion. Irrespective of religion the whole nation owns it. The second characteristic is, the epic story is always related to earth and heaven, and human beings and angels mix together. Several episodes and events cannot be believed by common man hence they are termed as myths in the west. However the epic instills greater values in humanity and they are found to be useful and essential for humanity. Even an iconoclast like Bernard Shaw acknowledged the essentialities of myths. In this country we know when the Absolute descends on earth and takes a form; it will have powers and super natural qualities. We have such experiences of several incarnations. No Indian doubts the events in these epics. Further, sages Valmiki and Veda Vyasa are the contemporaries of the heroes in these stories and actually witnessed all the events. Hence these epics are the living stories in this country and therefore they are well known Itihasas and not myths.

A good Christian would not feel offended when a historian says that Christ was born out of wedlock, but the Christian world believes in it even though there is no proof. In this connection, a passage from “Srimad Bhagavatham” published by Ramakrishna Math, Madras, and translated by Swami Tapasyananda is an apposite.

“Christ transcends history and becomes a Myth and Psychic Verity of world –wide significance. No man in his senses will offer to prove the above-cited statements as historical, but because they have been accepted by the psychic being of the community, they have a value far transcending the events if so-called history. The same is the case with Krishna and Rama. Their validity lies in the fusion their traditions have achieved with the supra-historical foundations of life.”

The almanac prepared by South India mentions that the month Jyestha Dasami is ‘Dasapapahara Dasami’ as ‘Sethu-bandhana Rameshwara Pratishta’ was done on that day.

It is self destructive to our national heritage to doubt the very birth of Sri Rama in this country and his journey from North to South, its perennial impact of this epic in this country. There is no surprise that the officer working under the present executive of the Central Government, filed an affidavit before the Supreme Court, that there is no proof of the birth of Sri Rama, as this epic is excluded from the curriculum of the educational system throughout the country. It is high time that we include these epics in the curricula as they represent the culture of this country. Indonesia, furnishes the best example, as in some of the universities in that country, Ramayana and Bharata are compulsory subjects, and when enquired, they assert that their faith is Islam, and their culture is Ramayana.

Lecture delivered by Justice P.Kodandaramayya, former Judge, A.P. High Court

On 125th Birth Day Celebrations of Pandit KOTA VENKATACHELAM

By Itihasa Sankalana Samithi (Bharateeya),

Andhra Pradesh

On 31-8-2010 at Telugu University, Hyderabad

My pranamams to Sri Sadguru Sivananda Murthy garu and the President Sri M.V.R.Sastry and other elders and esteemed friends seated on the dais and also seated facing the dais.

  • I must congratulate Itihasa Sankalana Samithi (Bharateeya) for having organized this 125th Birthday Celebrations of Brahma Sri Pandit Kota Venkatachelam. I read the introduction of their Chief Sri Sharat Hebalkar. He has got a comprehensive knowledge of the works of Pandit Kota Venkatachelam.
  • The word “Iti Ha Sa” – so indeed it was. Iti Ha Sa – heroic history. A particular history which actually happened like Ramayana and Mahabharatha and their dynasties. Purana is the history of the Universe. Chandogya Upanishads says Itihasa Puranam Pancamam. Having mentioned 14 vidyas – subjects, along with four Vedas it mentions Itihasa Puranam as the fifth. Hence, Sage Veda Vyasa said Itihasa Puranabhyam Vedam Samupa Bruhamete; By reading Ithasa Puranam only we can understand vedic hymns.
  • It is clear that the works of the Saint and Scholar Sri Venkatachelam disclose that deep knowledge of Sruti, Smruti, Purana Itihasas. I quote his statement:
    • “Unless we comprehend the creation of this universe, we cannot grasp the message of Vedic texts. It is for this reason that in all our scriptural texts, we find a place for the description of the scheme of creation. They include not only logic and grammar but also the science of medicine, the rules of conduct as well the scheme of creation are discussed along with the main topic of a book.”
  • Let us see the scheme of Upanisads. We shall take Taittriya Upanisad and Maha Narayana Upanisad. Taittiriya Upanisad mentions in Siksavali the rules of conduct we may call it dharma.
    • Speak the truth
    • Practice righteousness
    • Make no mistake about study
    • Offer desirable wealth to the teacher, do not cut off the line of progeny
    • Never be inadvertent about truth
    • Never be inadvertent about righteousness
    • Be always careful about protecting yourself
    • Do not neglect any activity relating to prosperity
    • Never be careless about learning and teaching
    • Never err in the duties towards Gods and Manes
    • Let mother be your Goddess
    • Let father be your God
    • Let teacher be your God
    • Let guest be your God
    • Works which are not blameworthy are to be resorted to not the others
    • Actions of our (Teachers) that are commendable are to be followed; not the others
    • Offer seat and remove fatigue of those vedic scholars who are more Praiseworthy
    • Give with dedication
    • Never offer with indifference
    • Offering must be in accordance with your prosperity
    • Offer with modesty
    • Offer with reverential fear
    • Offer with friendly gesture
    • If there is a doubt with regard to any righteous duty or custom or the method or the manner in which we have to deal with accused persons in a society, you consult Vedic scholars who are intelligent, experienced, independent, compassionate and righteous.
    • This is the injunction, this is the instruction, this is the secret of Vedas, this is the divine behest. All this is to be done thus and all this must be done thus. In Brahmanandavalli it mentions the creation from that Brahman indeed, which is this Self, was produced space. From space emerged air. From air was born fire. From fire was created water. From water sprang up earth. The last Bhruguvalli mentions self enquiry starting with second Anuvaka -- Annam Brahmeti Vyajanat. Prano Brahmeti Vyajanat. Mano Brahmeti Vyajanat. Vignanam Brahmeti Vyajanat. Anando Brahmeti Vyajanat. Again, from 7th to 10th Anuvakas it mentions dharma. Annam Na Nindyat. Thad Vratham. Never deprecate food, that should be the vow. Annam na paricaksita. Do not discard food. Annam Bahu Kurvita. You should make food plentiful. Na Kanchana Vasatho Pratyacaksita. You should not refuse anyone come for shelter, that should be the vow. Similarly, Maha Narayana Upanisad mentions both dharma and also enquiry into self. It start with truth Satyam Paramam Vadhanti – truth is the supreme means of liberation. It mentions Tapas, dama, sama, danam, dharmam, prajananam, Agni, Agnihotram, Yajnam, Manasam, at the end Nyasam means Sanyasam – is the supreme means of liberation.
  • Now I will take two works of Sri Venkatachelam which are going to publish into English.
    • Brahmanda Srusti Vijnanam – creation of universe
    • Manava Srishti Vignanam -- Genesis of the Human Race.
  • Cosmology, Cosmogony, Cosmography are the three words used with reference to the study of the Universe. Cosmology is the science of the formation of the Universe, consisting of five elements. Cosmogony is the study of the origin of the Universe, which can be described as the spiritual science. Cosmography is the description of the Universe and its constitution. The first book deals mainly with the third aspect of the Universe – cosmography. Our sages called the Universe as Brahmanda. We live in this Brahmanda – the golden egg. Under this subject, we have to study the seven upper worlds, the seven netherworlds, the Bhuloka consisting of seven dwipas and sub–divisions and the stellar region consisting of planets, stars, constellations, including the pole star and the great mountain known as Maha Meru. The author Sri Kota Venkatachalam, a Vedic scholar and a saint who participated in the national movement, in his Brahmanda Srusti Vijnanam broke a new ground and struck a different note. He based his conclusion on the strength of a Vedic hymn. Taitiriya Brahmana Kanda, Brahmanda Purana, Surya Siddhanta and Siddhanta Siromani, and stated his conclusions
    • The entire Universe was in the form of water in which was found a lotus leaf supported by a long stem. Prajapati in the form of a boar dipped into the water and discovered the earth at the root of the stem. He picked up a small fragment of clay and spread it on the lotus leaf which is termed as Pruthvi
    • ii.The earth was placed on the leaf is the globe on which we live – Bhugola. The earth from which the clay was brought to make Bhugola is Maha bumi.
    • The six dwipas and a portion of Jambu dwipa are in the Mahabumi and the Bhugola or the globe on which we live is only a portion of Jambu dwipa.
    • iv.The Jambu dwipa which is the globe on which we live alone was divided into nine varsas, but these varsas subsequently got degraded during the course of time.
    • With reference to the measure of time of Manvantaras, we are in the seventh Manvantara known as Vaivasvata Manvantara. The Manu who is ruling the present Manvantara is in the Bharat Khanda of Jambu dwipa portion of Mahabumi, along with sages.
     The author further said that the distinction found in the original puranas between Mahabumi and Bhuloka cannot be understood by foreigners because they are discovered at the level of soul. Professor R.L. Thompson, in his Mysteries of the Sacred Universe, appreciated the true account of cosmology given in Bhagavata Purana. However, he fairly stated at page 242 of his book that “we have not specifically studied the Vedic texts from a cosmological standpoint … … …”. The history is not history of globe alone. It is the entire universe. Similarly, geography also relates to entire universe of 14 worlds.

  • His work discloses knowledge, wisdom and bliss. He also gives the scientific knowledge for these three concepts. When we speak of science, we get the physical science, moral science and spiritual science. The study of five elements, we may call it, conveniently, as physical science. Moral science is the study of internal organs – mind, intellect, will and ego. The study of self is enquiry into self. That is the spiritual science. Scientists only take into account water, fire and air. It is Aristotle that included earth as an element. Even today, the scientists are not visualizing space as an element. They will stop with ether and beyond that they will say black matter.

    From the inception we have got the Vedic hymn saying “Prthivyapasthejo Vayurakasah”. Earth, Water, Fire, Air, Space and “Sabdha Sparsa Rupa Rasa Gandhah”. The subtle forms of the five elements of space, air, fire, water and earth are: South, Touch, Form, Taste, Scent, respectively.

    I shall mention the qualities of the gross forms of two elements Earth and Space. The qualities of gross forms of earth are immobility, weight, hardness, productiveness, scene, density, capacity to absorb all sorts of scents, cohesion, habitableness, and dhruti – the attribute of the mind, which is called patience. The qualities of gross forms of space are sound, extension, capacity of being enclosed, absence of refuge, power of being unmanifest and capacity for modification, incapacity for resistance, material cause for producing the sense of hearing, and the unoccupied parts of the human body. The quality of subtle forms of earth are scent, touch, taste, vision and sound. The scent is of nine sorts – pleasant, unpleasant, sweet, pungent, far-reaching, varied, dry, indifferent and Visada. The quality of suttled form of the space is sound alone. It is in seven forms i.e., Sadja, Risabha, Gandhara, Madhyama, Daivata, Pancama and Nisada.

    The author gives the spiritual sciences beyond space like consisting of three unmanifest powers (Manasa, Mahat and Ahamkara). Ahamkara is the common consciousness. Mahat is the common intellect, and Manasa is the cosmic energy with all its tendencies – Satva, Rajas and tamas. Though these concepts cannot be physically verified, their play in the universe can be clearly understood. A cow is gentle, horse is active and the tiger is cruel. Similarly, one man gentle, another emotional and a third cruel. Further, Sage Vedavyasa said the human body is a miniature of the universe consisting of five organs of sense, five organs of actions, five vital airs, and four internal organs.

    The author following the epic Mahabharata and other works of Sages, gives three lessons of Kala Manam – measure of time. Kala Mahima – Play of time. Kala Parinamam – quality of time (Yuga Dharma). It is astounding to note in the measure of time, apart for yugas and Kalpas. Figure ‘1’ followed by sixty two zeros have special names, namely Ogha and Maha ogha. Similarly, in the history of the universe, he gave several truths not known to humanity. He said the first creation is through Prakrti, five elements. The second creation is through the couple under procreation. He also said that in the ancient times, people got offspring by will, word of mouth, by sight and by touch and sexual union at a later period (Asramavasa Parva, Chapter 30). He also mentions instances of instantaneous birth described as Sadyogarbham, the conception immediately followed by birth.

    He gives the names of Earth as Prthvi, Medini, Kasyapi, Urvi and how it acquired these names with reference to ancient history several Kalpas back. He further mentions that all the five elements are living-beings. It was demonstrated in Ramayana as all five elements have appeared in human form. Further, the five organs of sense and the five organs of action have emanated from five elements. He also gives presiding deities of these both external and internal organs. Five organs of sense are, Sotra, Tvak, Chaksu, Jinhva and Ghrana – Ear, Skin, Eye, Tongue and Nose; the presiding deities respectively are, Space, Vidyut, Sun, Moon, Wind. Five organs of action are, Vak, Pani, Pada, Payu, Upastu – Speech, Hands, Legs, Anus and Sexual organ, the presiding deities respectively are, Fire, Indra, Visnu, Mitra and Prajapati. The four internal organs are, Mana, Budhi, Chitta, Ahankara – Mind, Intellect, Will and Ego and the respective presiding deities are, Moon, Brahma, Visnu (Upendra), Rudra.

    In second book he stated that time since creation began up to 1944 A.D. 195,58,85,044 with authoritative data. He establishes by evidence that the knowledge of Aryans is the base of Veda. They lived at on the banks of river Saraswathi known as Brahmavartadesa. Hence, he drew a map showing clearly Brahmarshi Desa, Madhya Desa, Aryavartha Desa, Yagna Desa and Bharata Varsha. This book clearly demonstrate that the history of this country must be re-written for restoring its glory.

  • Now I will mention what this great Saint said about Ramayana and Mahabharata. The History of Sri Rama belonging to the close of Treta Yuge in the twenty-fifth Maha Yuga(*) was given in the Epic known as Srimad Ramayana. The History of the war between Kurus and Pandavas towards the end of the last Dvapara Yuga was given in the Epic known as Bharatam. The dynasties of Kings from the beginning of creation, were described in that book. It gave not only the names of Kings and their descendents, but also the nature of human beings at that time and their habits, virtues and culture, education and government, spiritual traits, aspiration in the worldly and spiritual goals in great detail. (**) They were written in such a manner that, in spite of many disasters, many revolutions, many destructions by vandals, and many natural calamities, they could be memorized and preserved by the sages for posterity. Hence they are available to us even now. Thus, irrespective of dissolution and creation of universe they will continue to enlighten the humanity for ever even Kalpasatantarepi – hundreds and hundreds of Kalpas.

    (*) Brahmanda Purana mentions 24th Maha Yuga. We did not know which edition this author referred.

    (**)It is significant to note that every Indian almanac give the ruling period of Hariscandra and Nala, who were more ancient rulers than Sri Rama. The reference to Sri Rama alone was omitted. It is said that this was done under the pressure of foreign rulers.

  • I submit Pandit is not a mere scholar. He is a person has got Atmajnana (self knowledge). The Saint Scholar is not a mere philosopher, but he had practically lead life of a Saint and he met Sri Chandrasekhara Bharathi and Sri Chandrasekharendra Saraswathi and lived on fruits for 20 years traveled entire country by foot. His work “Advaita Bhodhini and Nirvicara Jeevanam” clearly establish, that he has practiced what he had learnt from sages and ultimately took the Sanyasasramam.
  • The knowledge given by him has become a forgotten knowledge. We have begun to disown our epics thus destroying our culture. It is imperative that the democracy must be spiritualized and every citizen must be prepared to sacrifice, for humanity which is the message of the Vedic hymns embodied in these two epics of Ramayana and Mahabharata. Instead of that, we are day-by-day conscience of our rights forgetting our duties. Hence, all citizens must read these two epics and imbibe the concepts embodied therein and sacrifice their self interest for humanity and make the country glorious, prosperous and ideal for the rest of the world to follow.

    • NATIONAL INJURY IF WE DENIGRATE HEROES OF RAMAYANA AND MAHABHARATA - ITIHASAS NOW TERMED AS EPICS

    I happened to see the journal THE WEEK dated Oct 27-2006. The article on the women of the epics is very interesting and instructive. However, having said that, the five of these epic women are reputed satis (chaste wives), two pieces were highlighted on page 46, stating “According to Bheel Mahabharata” Draupadi is not a pativrata (devoted to her husband), as she was raped by the serpent king Vasuki”. The very purpose of the article is destroyed by this statement. Secondly, another piece on page 45, stating that in Tamil poet Kamban’s version of Ramayana, Seeta is Ravana’s daughter, Ravana abducted her because he did not want her to suffer in the jungle.” I verified with some Tamil scholars. They said that this is shocking news and they are not aware of it. I do not know where these authors secured this. Apart from its correctness, it is contrary to the original text of Valmiki Ramayana.

    Further, there are incorrect and incomplete statements about the marriage of Draupadi, except the popular statement of five husbands and her suffering. It is stated, “The accepted version is that Kunti asked the brothers to share equally. Did Kunti think that a beautiful woman like Draupadi might have problems in being just Arjuna’s wife. Was Kunti afraid that she might become the cause of dissent among the brothers?” Kunti Devi was never guilty of such conduct. One should read the original epic even once before making any comment.

    Srimad Ramayana and Mahabharata are Iti-ha-sas. They are not strictly epics and certainly not mythologies. An epic is a long poem narrating the deeds of a hero or legendary figures. The legend is defined as traditional story popularly regarded as historical but unauthenticated; a myth. Mythology undoubtedly a myth which may be considered as not true; involving super natural or imaginary persons. It also includes fictitious persons.

    In fact, even epics also are very rare. Only few countries have epics. The famous epics of west are “The Iliad” and “The Odyssey” and Ramayana and Maha Bharata are the epics of India. Wordsworth in his Encyclopedia defines these epics as a foundation of nation or forging of national unity and often using religious or cosmological themes. The two major epic poems of west The Illiad and The Odyssey and also Ramayana and Maha Bharata and Anglo-Saxon Beowulf and the Finish Kalevala and also Bobylonian Gilgamesh. He classified Milton’s Paradise Lost, a secondary epic, whereas dictionaries classified it as epic.

    Thus, we seen, even assuming they are the myths, even iconoclast like Bernard Shah has acknowledged the essentiality of myths. These epics are not mere myths but they embody the national concept of history.

    The authors of Ramaya and Mahabharata, both Valmiki and Veda Vyasa respectively are contemporaries of the heroes of these two Iti-ha-sas, who have witnessed the events and recorded the same. Further, these two stories relate to Earth & Heaven. Chapter 17 Balakanda of Ramayana and Chapter 67 of Adiparva of Mahabharatha give the divine origin of principal characters in these stories.

    Let us take Ramayana first. The story is well known indisputably a stigma arises about the Chastity of Sita Devi when Ravana kept her in his custody for such a long time. Any amount of assertion, vow or evidence of any kind is of no avail to put the matter beyond human suspicion. When Veebhishana brought her before Srirama and when he saw her first: Srirama filled with Rosam, Harsamca Dainyamca – indignation, joy and felt miserable. “Indignation for the reason, how to accept her who lived with another person in his house under his control for a long time; joy for the reason he could see her at long last; felt miserable for the reason that he should abandon her forthwith without accepting her.

    In Ramayana, if this episode of “Test by fire” was not present, it would only have been a story of a hero full of valour, like the Greek epic story of “Lllaid” where a Trojan War was waged by Greeks to bring back the wife of Menelaus, a beautiful lady the “Helen”. The story did not end with this. The Ghost of suspicion in the minds of people was revived. Let us see the message of Sitadevi to Srirama when Laxmana left her at the hermitage of Valmaki, due to infamy as informed by Laxmana to her. She said “the censure of yours, and curse that has befallen on you shall indeed be averted by me also. Conduct yourself always among citizens, as you would do with your brothers. I do not feel penitent about my body. Do bear yourself in a manner through which the ill report among citizens is proved to be wrong.”

    Thus, it is seen that in practicing Dharma, Sita was no way inferior to her husband. Her mode of practice of Dharma is love. If Rama is personification of Dharma, she is personification of love or compassion. They have understood each other correctly, prepared to fulfill their respective duties in their lives, as an ideal couple, and ideal king and queen.

    In view of her suffering people would naturally think Srirama was cruel. That was what Laxmana and Sage Valmiki also felt. When Laxmana complained to Sumantra that he could not understand the conduct of Srirama in abandoning her knowing her to be innocent, Sumantra said That there was a secret and that was disclosed by Durvasa to your father. He said: “ Your son is a great person, he will live 11,000 years but he has to undergo exile along with his wife and he has to abandon his wife permanently and she will deliver twins in the forest. All this was due to the curse of Sage Brghu before the birth of your son on this earth”. On hearing this, Laxmana got the strength of mind and his delusion disappeared. On reaching Srirama he said to him “ if you grieve about your wife like this you will get another infamy, Rama is a weakling, he has abandoned his wife on the ground of unchastity, still he weeps for her. What is this pretence”. Rama stood firm in his abandonment of his wife and was mindful of his activity as a ruler.

    Now, we come to Valmiki as he began to doubt the greatness of Rama and his righteousness. Is he really Gunavan ? Is he really righteous? How long have I to wait? He waited 12 long years. Poor sage was dragged to the vortex of a householder under those circumstances unable to know how to solve the problem. Then Brahma Deva sent Sage Narada to Valmiki to narrate the story of Rama and after hearing the same and after the visit of Brahama deva, he composed the poem. When Srirama sent word through Valmiki that Sita should take again oath to prove her chastity, this time she took a different vow, if test by fire was repeated, people might think that she got some mantra or tantra to resist fire, she wanted to end her life and thus she stated “ If I have never been unfaithful in act, thought or speech to Srirama, Oh. Mother Earth, take me into your womb”. Thereupon the mother earth took her into her fold.

    The story of Ramayana is full of tears of Sita Devi and finally ends with her self crucification. That made the story a living story in our hearts as we are continuously sharing her grief. This is the secret of the great epic poem composed by Valmiki.

    If we see a drama, which is a tragedy, we grieve for the events in the scene and the emotion so caused in us acts as a cathartic agent and cleans our mind. Greeks call it catharsis. The mercy we feel about the scene begets pity. Our poets call it ‘Rasa’ and it is called Karuna rasa. The Valmiki composed this Kavya, which is of perennial value for humanity.

    So far as Rama is concerned, he loved Sita dearer than his life stating “Pranebyopi Gariyasi” He never looked at other man’s wife. Though he was a king, he maintained Ekapatnivrata, and earned great reputation throughout the world. We should also notice that Srirama & Sita Devi in their prime youth of 26 years and 18 years respectively observed Brahmachrya for a long period of 14 years, during the exile. When the fire God brought her from fire, announcing her chastity Sri Rama said “ I know she is pure. I kept quiet while she was entering fire in order to convince all the three worlds about her purity. Sita is not different from me just like the sunlight is from the sun. Sita Devi also said similarly about Srirama’s love towards her: She said: “Neither Srirama’s father, nor his mother, nor anyone else is equal, much less superior, to me in receiving his affection. Sri Rama is not different from me just like the Sunlight is from Sun. If Sri Rama is vain creature where do you get an ideal husband.

    Swami Vivekanada said: Rama and “Sita are ideals for the Indian Nation. Sita is the name in India for everything that is good, pure, and holy and for everything that we call womanly in woman. As Srimathi S.Janaki said in her article: She is paragon of virtues and observed “ If Rama is revered down the ages it is in no small measure due to the quiet and selfless role played by Sita. She was the woman behind this very successful man!” That was why Valmiki also said, Sitayacaritam Mahath - This is the great story of Sita Devi. The impact of Sitadevi on Indian woman is so great. Hence, Dr.Annie Beasant extolled the present Indian woman thus:- “ I bear you witness that I found nowhere spirituality so pure and lofty, devotion so strong and tender, purity so unsullied and spotless, patience so wise and beautiful as are found even in the modern women of India”. This is the great tradition that our country has achieved in our womanhood.

    We shall refer two characters in Ramayana. So far Kaikeyi is concerned she was more affectionate towards Srirama than her own son Bharata. When Mandhara announced the coronation of Srirama she was very happy and wanted to give a presentation to her for such a good news. However, Mandhara instilled hatred step by step in her mind and consequently the boons sought by her. Infact, sage Bharadwaja warned Bharata not to accuse his mother as there was a divine purpose of sending Srirama to exile.

    So far Ahalya was concerned, it is not correct to say that “Ahalya who made a conscious choice to fulfill her need and yet has been extolled ? the sage Goutama knew that Indra trapped her initially though later she also enjoyed his company and hence he restricted the curse so far she was concerned in the following terms.

    “ you will stay here for many thousands of years without food or drink, living on air (alone), and remain lying on ashes full of remorse. You will dwell in this hermitage unperceived by all created beings. When, however, Sri Rama, son of Dasaratha, visits this fearful grove, then (alone) you will be absolved (from your sin). Completely rid of cupidity and infatuation by virtue of hospitality that you will extend to him, O immoral woman, you will regain your own (pristine) body and return to my presence full of joy.”

    When Sri Rama entered her hermitage, his eyes fell on her, whose efflugence had been brightened by her austerities, who could not be easily perceived even by gods and demons, much less by earthly beings. Sri Rama and Lakshmana clasped her feet with joy on that occasion; while she in her turn welcomed both. There was abundant shower of flowers (from the heavens), accompanied by the beating of kettledrums by gods. And there followed a great rejoicing among Gandharvas and celestial nymphs. The gods heartily applauded the aforesaid Ahalya – who had been perfectly purified of body by dint of her austerities and (now) followed the will of Gautama – in the words “Good! Excellent!!” Chapter XLVIII of Bala-Kanda, Valmiki Ramayanam. We know even repentence purifies a sinful being. Now you see she was absolved of the sin because of greatest austerities which cannot be visualised in this age.

    With reference to Tara she was apsarasa they are instances where apsaras begot children through kings and sages and there is no prohibition for such conduct for apsaras. Now it is necessary to bare in mind that the story of Ramayana is beyond history. Though it is Itihasa but not epic or mythology as understood in the west, but when we called it mythology and free to comment as we please, there is no question of historian commenting about mythology. History as such has no special spiritual value, as events are only to a moment and not to the next. Events become history, to the extent they are remembered and become a tradition, and historical facts become spiritual reality to the extent they stimulate a Myth and become a Psychic Verity. Historian writes about Christ that it was said he was born outside wedlock and it was believed so. “Christ transcends history and becomes a Myth and a Psychic Verity of world-wide significance. No man in his senses will offer to prove the above cited statements as historical, but because they have been accepted by the psychic being of the community, they have a value for transcending the events of so-called history. The same is the case with Krishna and Rama. Their validity lies in the fusion their traditions have achieved with the supra-historical foundations of life”. The above exposition of the subject is given by Swamy Tapasyananda – in the general introduction to Srimad Bhagavata Volume –I published by Ramakrishna Math, Madras.

    This story of Ramayana took place several kalpas back, but not centuries. During the British Rule western pandits gave dates for everything and we cannot even rethink about them. Recently, it was found in NASA’s report that the Sethu constructed by Sri Rama for crossing the sea was visible and it could be estimated of the period of 17 lakhs of years. The earlier almanac used to state 1,25,69,103 as the Ravanabdas, and 1,25,58,031 as Sri Rama’s Rajyantabadas. This data was deliberately required to be deleted. We are now making the subjects of history, science and also language, questions of politics. Truth varies from place time and context. Are you justified in commenting about these characters in Srimad Ramayana that took place several lakhs of years ago as a cine director comments about a social picture.

    Let us advert to Maha Bharata. The story of Maha Bharata is very complicated. It is stated Durvigneyam Surirapi – difficult even for Gods to understand. It is a huge book. Seven times as great as Illiad and Odyssey combined. There are hidden facts not known generally. We must notice them. I will give reference to Maha Bharata translation by M.N. Dutt, Parimal Publications, Sanskrit Series-28, Re-print 1994.

    • Birth: Draupadi was Ayonija like Sitadevi. She was born from a sacred fire, not from mother’s womb. When she was born, an invisible voice was heard “This beauty of dark complexion will be the best of all women and she will be the cause of destruction of Kshatriyas. This lady of slender waist will in time accomplish the works of gods. From her will arise many fears (dangers) to the Kurus.” Adi Parva-Chapter-169, Stanzas 44, 48, 49.
    • Marriage: When Bhima and Arjuna after winning Draupadi at Swayamvaram, brought her to their mother. Usually, they used to bring the alms by begging. On that day also, they came and told their mother Kuntidevi that they obtained alms today. Kunti was inside a room, and did not see her sons. She, therefore, replied saying “enjoy all of you what you got.” thinking as usual alms of food. The moment she saw Draupadi, she said “Alas! What have I said” and she complained to Yudhistira, saying that “Your brothers represented to me that they brought alms; from ignorance, I said, “enjoy all of you”. Then tell me how my speech become futile and how Panchali should not become unhappy. (Adi Parva, Chapter-193, Stanzas 1 to 5).

      Thereupon, Yudhistira asked Arjuna to take Draupadi in marriage as he won her at the Swayamvara. Arjuna said, it is not virtue; you are the eldest and you should take her. Then all brothers looked at her. She too looked at them all. Glancing at the illustrious Krishna (Draupadi), they looked at one another, and taking their seats, they thought of her alone.” Seeing this, Yudhistira said, “This blessed Draupadi will be the wife of all of us.” All of them agreed and the matter was reported to King Drupada. He did not agree. Thereupon, Veda Vyasa came and he informed the birth story of Draupadi. The birth story contains multiple facts. Draupadi in her previous birth prayed Lord Siva for husband and having repeated praying for five times, he granted her five husbands. When she protested it, He said, all the five are one. The other facts are, the Indra who was ruling the Universe at that time, insulted Lord Siva. Thereupon, Lord Siva wanted him to go to earth and fulfill some divine purpose and born as a human being also said the previous Indras four of them should also take birth and those four should be helped by Devas like, Dharmaja, Vayu and also Aswini Devas and hence, all the five are only Indras. Vyasa gave divine eye to Drupada and he saw all of them resembling each other like Indra himself and he was fully satisfied and gave Draupadi in marriage to five of them successively one after another. It is also reported by Veda Vyasa that Draupadi is celestial Lakshmi of the partial Amsa of Sachidevi and she has been created by Brahma for that purpose. (All this is narrated in Chapter-199 of Adi Parva. It is not any commentators interpretation as wrongly mentioned by the author of the article in The Week).

      How the four Indras of previous birth were helped to take birth was clarified by Veda Vyasa in Markendeya Purana. Indra committed sins thrice. As a result of his killing Trisara, his vigour departed from the body of Indra and entered the body of God of Righteousness (yama). Similarly, when he killed Vrtra his strength of body departed and entered God of Wind. Further, when he enticed Gautama’s wife Ahalya, his splendour entered celestial physicians of Asvini Devas. Hence, the God of Righteousness, the God of Wind placed their powers so entered in their bodies from the body of Indra, in the womb of Kunti. Similarly, the celestial physicians placed their powers so entered from the body of Indra in the womb of Madri and they were born as Dharmaja, Bhyima, Nakula and Sahadeva. So far Arjuna is concerned, the then Ruler of Indra himself gave boon to the Kunti directly and as a result Arjuna was born. Thus, all of them have the same origin of Indra and Draupadi was the amsa of Sachi Devi and hence, there is no question of vulgarity in it. In the teeth of this, is there any justification to comment that she has no choice of selection of her husband.It is also reported that she was given in marriage to each of these brothers day by day in succession and it is recorded that she regained her virginity day after day everyday after the previous marriage. As per the advise of Narada, they used to enjoy her by yearly turns. Further, each brother had separate wives. Arjuna had four wives.

    • The war of Maha Bharata is a divine plan. The incident of stripping of clothes of Draupadi in open assembly constitutes the main reason for the war. Duryodhana also, in fact, said more than ones that Pandavas could not forget the insult hurled against Draupadi and their owe of revenge. It was difficult to reconcile and live in peace together. It is true, she has suffered onslaught on her person by Jayadrada, Keechaka and every incident has aggravated her vengeance to wage war.

      It is true that the question raised by her “whom did Yudhistira lose first – himself or me is very pertinent. But, Duryodhana made a trick. He said, let Yudhistira say that she was not properly won. Yudhistira was silent. Hence, Bhishma also said, it is difficult for me to answer this in view of the silence of Yudhistira. Yudhistira already said I was won and his brothers also accepted that. When her husbands have become servants, they are bound by the direction of their master. The wife of the defeated servants cannot bring greater misery to their husbands, irrespective of the fact that whether she was properly won or not. Further, she has got abiding faith in her husbands valour and also their virtue and hence, she also thought at that time Yudhistira must be right in not expressing his view and hence she kept quiet and suffered. Apart from this, the participants, in the assembly including Bhishma and Drona could have said that even though she was won rightly, you cannot molest her and should have protested against such onslaught. Why should they keep quiet, as the master cannot molest the wife of a servant or a woman who is a servant. A failure to protest against this conduct is again the reason that a devil entered their mind, so that this instance of stripping the clothes should take place and the consequence owe of Bhima to kill Dushasana and Duryodhana should occur. We cannot ascribe this to the inefficiency of husbands, but their abiding faith on Dharma.

    Other hidden facts:
    • When the stripping of clothes took place, bad omens occurred. Thereupon, Gandhari rushed to Drutarastra and warned him of the consequences. Drutarastra gave two boons to Draupadi to make her husbands free from bondage. When she refused to take third boon, he gave the entire kingdom and everything they lost in the dice and asked them to go back and be happy and forget everything. When Duryodhana wept over the situation and requested his father to again summon them, he again sent word directing “to bring back Pandavas, even if they have gone a great way off. Let them come and throw dice once again. (Chapter-64, Sabha Parva). Here is another hidden fact.

      After Maya Sabha was constructed, Sage Narada came and told Yudhistira that he saw his father King Pandu and he wanted him to convey his desire of your performing Raja Suya Yaga and if you performs the Raja Suya Yaga, he will also live in the assembly of Indra for a long time like, Harishchandra and other kings. However, when Yudhistira inclined to obey the command of his father, the sage warned him “On the commencement of such sacrifice, a war may break out which may destroy the Kshatryas, and which may be the cause of the total destruction of the whole world. A slight obstacle (to it) may cause the ruin of the whole earth. (Chapter-12, Sabha Parva). After the death of Sisupala in Raja Suya Yaga, Yudhistira asked Veda Vyasa whether the calamity consequence of Raja Suya Yaga was terminated. Veda Vyasa said, “for thirteen years those portents will produce great results. O king, they may even cause the destruction of all the Kshatryas. “O best of the Bharata race, O descendant of Bharata, in course of time, making you the sole cause, the assembled Kshatriya kings will be destroyed for the fault of Duryodhana and the prowess of Bhima and Arjuna.” (Chapter-46, Sabha Parva). Thereupon, in the presence of his brothers, Yudhistira took a vow saying that, “O children, O blessed ones, listen to the vow I make from this day. For what other purpose am I to live for thirteen years? I shall not speak a harsh word to my brothers or to any of the kings of the earth. I shall remain obedient to my relatives and practise virtue. If I live in this way, making no distinction between my own sons and those of others, there will be no disagreement in the world. Disagreement is the cause of war. O best of men, I shall keep war at a distance, and I shall ever do what is agreeable to others. Thus no evil reputation will touch me in the world.”

      That was the reason why Yudhistira obliged to go and play dice second time after the episode of stripping the clothes. Why should brothers accompany him. Even Draupadi accompanied them. They know his vow. This was also the reasons why Yudhistira was prepared to take five villages to avoid war.

    • We have got another crucial fact not known to all. When the Pandavas were undergoing exile, Duryodhana and Karna went to the forest to insult Pandavas. Unfortunately, they met with Gandharvas and were bound by them. Thereupon, Yudhistira requested both Bhima and Arjuna to release them, though they refused to do so initially. After they brought back, Duryodhana, Yudhistira released them and asked them to go to house comfortably and not to do hazardous things stating that when others invade us, we were all 105. On that, Duryodhana felt insulted and entered the water to die. There, Danavas and Raksas took him to netherworld and promised him to help and not to die. They also said that the soul of their leader Narakasura who died, entered Karna and he will help you. Not only that we will enter the Bhishma and Drona also and they will help you. That is why Karna who was born to Sun God and a Kshatriya lady Kunti became responsible for the war. It is he who prompted Dussasana to unrobe the clothes saying she is not a Pativrata who married five husbands and also exhorted her to marry some of the brothers of Duryodhana or Duryodhana himself as her husbands were useless fellows. Thereupon, Duryodhana showed his thy and asked her to come and occupy. This provoked Bhima to take two vows of killing them.
    • The warriors have developed split personalities. Bhishma, such a pious man, said he will not kill Pandavas, but kill army and waged war for 10 days and even prepared to give clue for his death. Similarly, Dronacharya. Warrior Karna also when reported by Sri Krishna that he was a son of Sun God, he said, “I know that Yudhistira will succeed. He is a pious man. He should succeed. Please do not tell this to anyone.” He also expressed his regrets about what he said about Pandavas, saying it was only to please his friend Duryodhana. Knowing fully well, they could not succeed, he gave false hope to Duryodhana always. Similarly, Salya, having joined the army of Duryodhana in war, promised Yudhistira to help him while being the charioteer of Karna.
    • In Drona Parva, Arjuna made an enquiry with Veda Vyasa, that whenever he got into his chariot, a huge power going before him, what was that. He said, it is Lord Siva who was killing a devouring people even before you kill them.
    • Sauptika Parva also mentions that Aswadhama killed all warriors who were asleep. It is not possible to kill so many people even when they were sleeping by single hand. Sri Krishna said, it is Lord Siva by entering Aswardhama who did all that, but not Aswadhama himself.
    • In Stree Parva, Veda Vyasa while consoling Drutarashtra said (Chapter-8), said that he saw mother earth in her embodied form in the assembly of Indra, complaining her burden. Thereupon, Lord Vishnu replied that the eldest son of Drutarashtra namely, Duryodhana will accomplish your work. Through that king your object will be achieved. For his sake, many kings assemble together on the field of Kuru, will cause one another to be killed and your object will be achieved quickly. He was born of Kali Amsa. Sakuni and Karna will help him and hence this war is destined and it is a plan of devas and Pandavas are not to be blamed.
    • While referring to Maha Prastana in Swargarohana Parva, a comment was made about husbands not doing justice. It is an unfair comment. In Maha Prastana parva, while Yudhistira was going along with brothers and Draupadi, one after one fell down. Bhima asked the reason. Sahadeva was proud of his wisdom; Nakula was proud of his beauty. Arjuna was proud of his valour. Bhima was proud of his strength and Draupadi was partial towards Arjuna. In Swargarohana Parva, for whatever the reason, we found all of them in hell and Yudhistira was also taken to hell for his sin of uttering falsehood to his Guru Dronacharya. When he complained about the perversity of the destiny of his brothers and wife at hell, it is reported that people who first endures hell, must afterwards enjoy the celestial region. Your brothers have to suffer a few minutes this hell and subsequently go to heaven. Hence, it is seen they have to suffer for some time in the hell. There is no question of husbands hegemony in this case.
    • The other criticism of this epic suffered, interpolations and didactic material is a old song, sung number of times. On this question one should refer ‘On the meanings of Maha Bharata by V.S. Sukthankar, who carefully, meticulously devoted a chapter of 35 pages on Maha Bharata and critics and the reason for this didactic material. Mainly it is instructive, but to the modern young man and foreigners, it appears to be repetition and and tediously pedantic also. Sri Aurobindo remarked that the whole nation wrote it. The remark is fully justified for the reason that Maharsi Veda Vyasa collected the utterances of several sages on different concepts and precepts long prior to his composition and hence it is said it is the work of several hands and of different periods. Sri Vivekananda said that this book is not read properly either inside the country or outside the country. Aurobindo remarked one should closely read and analyse the heterogeneous material and compare with Ramayana and Puranic and Classical Literature.
    • Now we see that Maha Bharata was built with Santhi Rasa. The entire epic is said to be of Santi Rasa. Scholars say that Nirveda-disinterestedness in worldly affairs achieved through real self knowledge gives santirasa (Tranquility). Hence, the epic story consists of innumerable discourses and upakhyanas consisting philosophical and spiritual values, always diverting the attention of the reader from the main story such as Bhagavadgita, Vidura Niti, Sanasujatiyam, Anugita and the extensive stories of Ramayana, Savitri and Damayanti. At the end, all persons related to Kauravas and Pandavas were killed. Hence, Pandavas cannot claim to be conquerers to enjoy their conquest and, the epic story records how the Pandavas also abandoned the kingdom and voluntarily started their journey leaving their mortal coil. Three fourths of Maha Bharata consists of Upakyanas and discourses and 1/4th alone is the story. Hence, it is clear that the object of the author is quite different. It aims at santi rasa. It is right to say that “ greed devoured with them not the throne of Hastinapura ”. This story of Mahabharata tells about greedy nature of man whereas Ramayana speaks of kama - lust of man.

      Mahabharata records where a woman regains virginity after giving birth to a child. The earliest instance was one Madhavi gave birth to four sons through different persons. Similarly, Satyavati, Kunti and also Draupadi regained virginity. In ancient times, people got offspring by will, word of mouth, by sight and by touch and sexual union came at a later period. We have seen the mother earth embodied form. Sage Vedavyasa said the five elements are live beings. Space is Brhma – God - the infinite. In Asramavasikaparva Kunti lamented about the birth of Karna when she was maiden. Vedavyasya consoled her saying might is right, anything done by mighty people is considered to be pure, meritorious and cannot be questioned. She described how the sun god came to her. She said “ He divide himself into two parts, with one portion he was in the sky, and with the other he stood on the Earth before me. With one he heated the worlds and with another he came to me”. When she requested that she committed a mistake and he may go back, he refused and threatened to curse the sage and herself. Thereafter, she said, “let me have a son like you”. She said “the deity of thousand rays then entered me with his energy and stupefied me completely”. He then said “you will have a son”. She said “I continued to live in the inner apartments and desirous of saving the honour of my Father, I cast into the waters my infant son named Karna who thus came into the world secretly”. She also said “Forsooth, through the grace of that god, I once more became a virgin“ (Ashramavasikaparva Chapter 30). However, begetting the child before marriage was prohibitive then, as well as now even in future, no society can accept that. Dr.Prema Kasturi correctly stated the dignity in suffering of Kunti Devi.

      The epic also records Sadygarbha – conception and birth simultaneously as in the case of Veda Vysya by Satavathi. On his birth, with the permission of his mother, he adopted asceticism. He went away saying “as soon as I shall be remembered by you on any occasion I shall immediately appear before you” (Adiparva Chapter 63). When we comment about these stories you must take into account all the facts related with those deities or those sages. Hence, it is unfair to comment without taking into consideration these factors and describing that society as a feudal and the literature as a bardic literature.

      Further, it is incorrect to state that it is a case of epic converted into religious books. It is the converse case. In fact, both Ramayana and Maha Bharata are religious books. That is why our Government is feeling diffident to introduce them in the curriculum. They are related to earth and heaven. We believe that they are true stories. The characters are divine. So far, Ramayana is concerned, Rama is Maha Visnu and his consort Sita is Lakshmi Devi. Ramayana is read as a religious book every day. So far Maha Bharata is concerned, it consists of the divine song Bhagavad Gita and several holy recitations, including Visnu Sahasra Namam, Siva Sahasranamam. The heroes of both the epics were adored. Sri Rama as personification of Dharma, Vigrahavan Dharma, and the other is Punya Sloka (Yudhistira). We can visualize the national injury, if we denigrate the heroes of these epics.

      The first epic Ramayana gives two basic virtues of filial devotion and matrimonial purity. All religious books explore the purity called, matrimonial purity. Bible enjoins that it is good for man not to touch the woman; the wife does not have authority over her body but the husband does – and the likewise the husband does not have authority over his body, but the wife does. Whoever looks at a woman with lust has already committed adultery with her in his heart.

      Husband and wife should remember soul has no sex. Both of them should over grow the sex awareness for the sake of progeny. Again a comment was made the rule of begetting a son. It is ordained duty of couple to beget progeny. In their old age, both husband and wife require protection. The feminists must make distinction between the cruelty of husband and offence of third party. So far the cruelty of husband is concerned, it is a family law. Initially, we must give our girl children, good education and ability to stand on their feet if any cruelty has to be met by her husband. Both should show fidelity to each other. Other infirmities or angularities, the feminists should not interfere. They should settle themselves. If the feminists begin to interfere and criticize every husband, the whole country will be divided vertically between men and women and that is destructive of the very interest of women. Let us remember husband associations are being formed already to oppose the feminist movement.

      The more urgent need of the nation about women is, protecting them against onslaughts of third parties. It is reported that ‘Trafficking of women from Assam on the rise – every year an average of 250 women and 200 girl children go missing in Assam.’ Female foetus are being destroyed. Father is reported guilty of rape against his daughter. We hear harassment of women in offices and workshops, Gang Rapes are reported. It is also reported in Saudi Arabia women changed their sex to avoid male domination. We have reached a stage of requesting licenses for sex services.

      Each man should look upon women except his wife, as his mother, sister or daughter. The principle of equality is inapplicable between women and men. The feminists should advocate worship of women by men, instead of mere equality between them. We have in this country such practice of adoring the divinity as mother. Smriti declared where women were worshipped, there gods dwell. Every woman should develop their internal strength to protect their chastity. Swami Vivekananda said, “What brutality is their which purity and chastity cannot conquer?

      Woman is mother, her burden is more than man; in that sense the modern woman can be compared with the heroines of these epics. The woman sacrifices and they achieve eternity by there sacrifice.

      The feminists cannot try to discover cruel husbands in the heroes of these Epics. It is a futile attempt and most injurious to the nation.

    NOTE TO SUPREME COURT ADVOCATE WITH REFERFNCE TO SETHUBANDA CASE:

    On the question of construction of Sethu by Sri Rama, Sage Valmiki in his work Srimadramayanam in Chapter 22 of Yudhakanda refers the same in great detail. It is stated, it was constructed five days and the extent of construction by each day and how it was completed on the fifth day and how it was witnessed by Devas from above, please refer Page 1444 Gita Press, Part-III stanza 68 to 89.

    The author of the epic Valmiki is the contemporary of the story, the contemporary of the Hero of Ramayana. He knows the events in the epic. He recorded them. No other proof is necessary when the authentic version of the author is available. If after 10,000 years, we would like to know the events of II World War, it is enough if we read the work of W.Churchil. No other proof is necessary.

    We also give further data on this topic.
    • Brahmanda Puranam mentions Sri Rama was born in Treta Yuga of 24th Maha Yuga. If we calculate as per that data, we arrive at 1,81,60,100 by 1999 A.D., refer page 200 of Justice Kodanda Ramaiah, the Light of Ramayana.
    • About the age of Ramayana refer the introduction in the Message of Mahabharata, page xxxix. The old almanac prepared in earlier period used to state as 1,25,69,103 as Ravanabdas and 1,25,58,031 as Sri Rama’s Rajyantabadas (years). If we deduct the later figure from earlier figure of Ravanbdas, it comes to 11,000 years which was a total number of years of Sri Rama’s rule that clearly synchronizes with the period mentioned by the poet Valmiki in the epic.
    • The present almanac mentions even today the end of Rule of Harichandra and also King Nala as 2,38,24,249 and 1,36,13,956 respectively. But the end of rule of Sri Rama was deliberately omitted on the pressure of foreign rulers. Only recently, one Scholar Madhura Krishna Murthy included end of the rule of Srirama Chandra as 1,25,58,107.
    • Scandapurana mentions that Sri Rama constructed after the consent of the Sea-God with the help of monkey warrior Nala. It was constructed on Jyesta Sudha Dasami, on that day Sri Rama also adored Lord Shiva and it was considered to be pious day known as Sethubandana Rameswara Pratista’.
    • The almanac mentions even today Sethubandana Rameswara Pratista’ as a pious day and the almanac of 2007 mention that falls on 26th May, 2007.
    • Journal Jyothisya Vignanam at page 4, released on 10.04.2008, 44-3, mentions the Scandapurana in detail and also refers Sethubanda Rameswara Patista as a pious day.
    • Almost all Puranas mentions Ramayana and the construction of Sethu.
    • Mahabharata mentions entire story of Ramayana in Vanaparva extensively named it as separate Sub-Parva as Ramopakshayana Parva from Chapter 272 to 291. In Vanaparva, Vol-II, Chapter 282, stanza 44, page 406 of M.N.Dutt, Parimal Publications states that the descendant of Kakustha by this means caused a bridge ten yojanas broad and hundred yojanas long to be erected over the sea. Yojana according to the earliest almanac means 4.91 miles i.e., 4 miles 7 furlongs 61.6 yards.
    • Shanti Parva, Chapter 29 also mentions Sri Rama among 16 great kings and mentions that he ruled the Kingdom 11,000 years.
    • People through out the country go to Rameswaram and take bath at Sethu where Rama is said to have prayed initially Sea-God. The place where the people take bath is east to the temple is nearly six miles to the temple of Rameswaram. Near that temple there is a Mahendragiri. Further 30 miles thereof to the Ramanathapuram, to the north of that 10 miles known as Navapasana where Rama is said to have installed 9 Grahas and 7 miles extent of it known be Dharbasayanam. All these places are considered to be holy and the Sri Rama was said to have traveled from North to South, visited several places and throughout the country there are temples constructed and people adore him.
    • There is a story spread throughout the country Uduta Bhakti, the story of squirrel, where the squirrel tried to help Sri Rama by quenching herself in the sand and leaving the sand touched to its body on the Sethu , thus bringing some sand to the Sethu construction. Sri Rama was said to be pleased with and drew his hands on his back, those stripes are visible on squirrel and that is believed to be at the instance of Sri Rama showing his love to that small being.

      • This data shows the story is a pre-historic if not supra-historic. It is not capable of proof of events mentioned therein. In fact, this epic is cosmological as it related to earth and heaven as human beings on earth and angles from heaven are mixed up, that is the meaning of cosmological as the encyclopedia of Wordsworth mentions at Page 744, Vol-II, Helicon Publishing Ltd, 1995.

      It is significant to note that in Indian epics are not myths as understood in the West. They are ithihasas. Itihasa means iti-ha-a-sa- means (so indeed it was) it is a history, tradition account of former events historic (M.M.William). Every Indian accepts the epic story as true as they know the incarnations of the absolute descending on earth. In the West they are considered as myths as they are not capable of being proved though they are believed to be as true even though unauthenticated.

      A passage from introduction to SriMadbhagavatham is relevant in this connection as these pre-historic events which is the part of nation’s history cannot be subject matter of any proof or enquiry about the truth.

      Sri Tapasyananda in Vol-I Srimad Bhagavatha mentions about this aspect which is relevant to this regard may be noted. Under the heading Supra-historic validity of Puranas, page xliv, “History as such has no special spiritual value, as events are only at the moment and are not in the next. Events become history, as stated before, to the extent they are remembered and become a tradition, and historical facts become spiritual reality to the extent they stimulate a Myth and become a Psychic Verity. For example, in itself the much vaunted historicity of Christ gives no spiritual significance to that religion, but its Christ Myth does.
